A_{ST}^{(2)}=0
, at the leading level, we have

(4.66)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha})(I-H_{0})\zeta_{ST}^{(3)}=

\displaystyle-2b_{ST}^{(2)}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{t}(I-H_{0})\zeta_{ST}^{(1)}+G_{ST,2}^{(3)}-(\partial_{t}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha})(I-H_{0})\zeta_{ST}^{(1)}

(4.67)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4(-1)(i)(i\omega)\zeta_{ST}^{(1)}+2|\zeta_{ST}^{(1)}|^{2}\zeta_{ST}^{(1)}+4\omega^{(2)}\zeta_{ST}^{(1)}

(4.68)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(4\omega^{(2)}-2)\zeta^{(1)}_{ST}.

Therefore, one has

(4.69)

(\partial_{t}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha})(I-H_{0})\zeta_{ST}^{(3)}=(4\omega^{(2)}-2)\zeta^{(1)}_{ST}.

Plugging (
4.64
) into the equation (
4.69
), duo the holomorphicity, we obtain

(4.70)

\omega^{(2)}=\frac{1}{2}.

From all computations above, we conclude

(4.71)

\omega=1+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{3}).

and

(4.72)

\zeta^{(1)}_{ST}=ie^{i\alpha+\omega t},\quad\zeta_{ST}^{(2)}=i,\quad\zeta_{ST}^{(3)}=\frac{i}{2}e^{-i\alpha-\omega t}.

We finish the proof of Proposition
4.3
.

4.3.4.
Approximation of the Stokes wave

Using the
\zeta_{ST}^{(j)}
obtained above, we are able to defined an approximate form of the Stokes wave.

Define the approximation of the Stokes wave
\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}
as

(4.73)

\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}=\alpha+\epsilon ie^{i(\alpha+\omega t)}+\epsilon^{2}i+\epsilon^{3}\frac{i}{2}e^{-i\alpha-i\omega t}.

Then by construction, we know that

\displaystyle|\zeta_{ST}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}|=O(\epsilon^{4}).

Here we note that in
\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}
only one non-trivial fundamental frequency is involved. This fact will be crucial in the analysis.

5.
Multiscale analysis and derivation of the NLS from the full water waves

In this section, we shall use the water waves system (
3.1
) to perform the multiscale analysis and derive the NLS.

5.1.
Basic setting

Given a Stoke wave of amplitude
\epsilon
from Proposition
1.1
and Section §
4
, by Proposition
4.3
, it has the following expansion in terms of
\epsilon
:

(5.1)

\displaystyle{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,t)=

\displaystyle\alpha+\epsilon\zeta^{(1)}_{ST}+\epsilon^{2}\zeta^{(2)}_{ST}+\epsilon^{3}\zeta^{(3)}_{ST}+\dots

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha+i\epsilon e^{i(\alpha+\omega t)}+\epsilon^{2}i+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{3}e^{-i(\alpha+\omega t)}+\dots.

Consider the solution to the system (
3.1
) as a perturbation of the Stokes wave above in
H^{s}({q\mathbb{T}})
. Expanding the solution in terms of the power of
\epsilon
, we seek for
\zeta(\alpha,t)
of the form

(5.2)

\zeta(\alpha,t)=\alpha+\epsilon\zeta^{(1)}(\alpha,t)+\epsilon^{2}\zeta^{(2)}(\alpha,t)+\epsilon^{3}\zeta^{(3)}(\alpha,t)+...

where
\zeta^{(1)}
satisfies the ansatz

(5.3)

\zeta^{(1)}=B(\alpha_{1},t_{1},t_{2})e^{i(\alpha+\omega t)},\quad\quad\alpha_{1}:=\epsilon\alpha,t_{1}:=\epsilon t,t_{2}:=\epsilon^{2}t.

for some periodic function
B
on
q_{1}\mathbb{T}
wtih
q_{1}:=\epsilon q
from the view of the modulational approximation and the long-wave perturbation.

Since
\zeta
is the perturbation of
\zeta_{ST}
, from the coefficient of
\epsilon
in (
5.1
), we will choose

\quad\quad B=i(1+\psi).

Eventually, we will show that in order make the expansion (
5.2
) valid,
B
will admit the form
B(X,T)
with
X=\epsilon(\alpha+\frac{1}{2\omega}t)
,
T=\epsilon^{2}t
and solve

iB_{T}+\frac{1}{8}B_{XX}+\frac{1}{2}|B|^{2}B-\frac{1}{2}B=0.

In the remaining part of this section, we first give some estimates for almost holmorphicity of wavepackets which will be useful to handle functions with slow variables. With these preparations, we then analyze the expansion for
A-1
,
b
from (
3.1
) and find
\zeta^{(1)}
,
\zeta^{(2)}
,
\zeta^{(3)}
in (
5.2
).

5.2.
Almost holomorphicity of wavepackets

Let
\lambda
be an integer, we know that
e^{i\lambda\alpha}
is the boundary value of the holomorphic function
e^{i\lambda(\alpha+iy)}
in the lower half plane. If
\lambda<0
, then
e^{i\lambda(\alpha+iy)}\rightarrow 0
as
y\rightarrow-\infty
which implies that
(I-H_{0})e^{i\lambda\alpha}=0
. In general, if
f\in H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})
,
\|(I-H_{0})fe^{i\lambda\alpha}\|_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}
can be comparable with
\|f\|_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}
. However, given
m\in\mathbb{N}
, if
f\in H^{s+m}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})
, then
\|(I-H_{0})f(\epsilon\alpha)e^{i\lambda\alpha}\|_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}
is as small as
\epsilon^{m-1/2}
.
2
2

2

Recall that
q_{1}=\epsilon q
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
be given. Let
f\in H^{s}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then

(5.4)

\|(I+\text{sgn}(\lambda)H_{0})f(\epsilon\alpha)e^{i\lambda\alpha}\|_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{m-1/2}\|f\|_{H^{s+m}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})},

where
C
depends on
s
only.

Proof.

We consider the case that
\lambda<0
. By the Fourier series on
q_{1}\mathbb{T}
, we write

(5.5)

f(\alpha)=\frac{1}{2\pi q_{1}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{k}e^{ik\frac{\alpha}{q_{1}}},

where

(5.6)

a_{k}=\int_{-q_{1}\pi}^{q_{1}\pi}f(\alpha)e^{-ik\frac{\alpha}{q_{1}}}\,d\alpha.

Therefore, one has

(5.7)

f(\epsilon\alpha)e^{i\lambda\alpha}=\frac{1}{2q_{1}\pi}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{k}e^{i(2k/q-|\lambda|)\alpha}.

By Parseval’s identity, it follows

(5.8)

\|f\|_{H^{\ell}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}^{2}=\frac{1}{2q_{1}\pi}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{|k|^{2n}}{q_{1}^{2n}}a_{k}^{2}.

Notice that for
2k<|\lambda|q
, we have
(I-H_{0})a_{k}e^{i(2k/q-|\lambda|)\alpha}=0
.
Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert(I-H_{0})fe^{-i|\lambda|\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2q_{1}\pi)^{2}}\left\lVert(I-H_{0})\sum_{2k=q|\lambda|}^{\infty}a_{k}e^{i(-|\lambda|+2k/q)\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{q}{q_{1}^{2}\pi^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{s}\sum_{2k=q|\lambda|}^{\infty}(-|\lambda|+2k/q)^{2n}a_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{q}{q_{1}^{2}\pi^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{s}\sum_{2k=q|\lambda|}^{\infty}(2k/q)^{2n}(k/q_{1})^{-2(m+s-n)}(k/q_{1})^{2(m+s-n)}a_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q}{q_{1}^{2}\pi^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{s}\sum_{2k=q|\lambda|}^{\infty}\Big((k/q_{1})^{2n}\epsilon^{-2n}\Big)(k/q_{1})^{-2(m+s-n)}(k/q_{1})^{2(m+s-n)}a_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q}{q_{1}^{2}\pi^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{s}\epsilon^{-2n}\sum_{2k=q|\lambda|}^{\infty}(k/q_{1})^{-2(m+s-n)}(k/q_{1})^{2(m+s)}a_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{q}{q_{1}^{2}\pi^{2}}\epsilon^{-2m}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(k/q_{1})^{2(m+s)}a_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{2m-1}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{H^{s+m}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

as desired. Here in the last step, we used the estimate

(5.9)

\epsilon^{-2n}(k/q_{1})^{-2(m+s-n)}\leq\epsilon^{2m+(2s-2n)}\leq\epsilon^{2m}

for
k\geq|\lambda|q/2
. We are done.
∎

5.3.
Multi-scale expansion

In this subsection, we compute the multi-scale expansion. The general strategy here is similar to
[
66
,
62
]
(§3 of
[
66
]
, §4 of
[
62
]
).
We note that the nonlinear Schrödinger equation has not been derived in the setting with Stokes wave. Also notice that under the current setting, in the leading order
Be^{i\alpha+i\omega t}
,
\omega
can depend on
\epsilon
, which is different from previous works, for example,
[
66
,
62
]
.

Our goal is to choose
\zeta(\alpha,t)
such that

\zeta(\alpha,t)=\alpha+\epsilon\zeta^{(1)}+\epsilon^{2}\zeta^{(2)}+\epsilon^{3}\zeta^{(3)}+O(\epsilon^{4})

and
3
3

3

See Proposition
4.3
.

(5.10)

\displaystyle\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,t)=

\displaystyle\alpha+i\epsilon e^{i(\alpha+\omega t)}+\epsilon^{2}i+\frac{i}{2}\epsilon^{3}e^{i(-\alpha-\omega t)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha+\epsilon\zeta^{(1)}_{ST}+\epsilon^{2}\zeta^{(2)}_{ST}+\epsilon^{3}\zeta^{(3)}_{ST}

and the followings hold

(1)

\zeta
solves (
3.19
).

(2)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\bar{\zeta}-\alpha)=0
.

(3)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})D_{t}\bar{\zeta}=0
.

(4)

\|\alpha+\epsilon\zeta^{(1)}+\epsilon^{2}\zeta^{(2)}+\epsilon^{3}\zeta^{(3)}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}\delta
.

By Corollary
A.1
,
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\bar{\zeta}-\alpha)=0
and
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})D_{t}\bar{\zeta}=0
imply

(5.11)

\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\zeta_{\beta}(\bar{\zeta}(\beta,t)-\alpha)\,d\beta=0,\quad\quad\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\zeta_{\beta}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}(\beta,t)\,d\beta=0.

In this section, we will use the notation
\phi:=\alpha+\omega t
.

5.3.1.
O(\epsilon)
Level

Plugging (
5.2
) into the the momentum equation, the first equation of (
3.1
), we know that
\zeta^{(1)}
satisfies
(\partial_{t}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha})\zeta^{(1)}=O(\epsilon^{3})
because

(5.12)

\omega=1+O(\epsilon^{2})

by construction.

5.3.2.
O(\epsilon^{2})
Level

Using the system (
3.1
) again, we need

(5.13)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(2)}=-(2\partial_{t_{0}}\partial_{t_{1}}-i\partial_{\alpha_{1}})(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(1)}+(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})H_{1}\zeta^{(1)}.

Note that by (
2.30
) and the explicit choice of (
5.3
), one has

\displaystyle(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})H_{1}\zeta^{(1)}=

\displaystyle(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}Be^{i\phi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})[\zeta^{(1)},I+H_{0}]Be^{i\phi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(\epsilon^{4})

where in the last step we applied Lemma
5.1
. To avoid secular terms, we choose
\zeta^{(1)}
such that

(5.14)

-(2\partial_{t_{0}}\partial_{t_{1}}-i\partial_{\alpha_{1}})(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(1)}=0.

Using Lemma
5.1
again, we have
(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(1)}=2\zeta^{(1)}+O(\epsilon^{4})
. So plugging the ansatz, (
5.3
), into (
5.14
), it follows

B_{t_{1}}-\frac{1}{2\omega}B_{\alpha_{1}}=O(\epsilon^{4}).

So we choose
B=B(X,T)
, with

(5.15)

X=\alpha_{1}+\frac{1}{2\omega}t_{1}=\epsilon(\alpha+\frac{1}{2\omega}t),\quad T=t_{2}=\epsilon^{2}t.

To choose
\zeta^{(2)}
, we use
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\bar{\zeta}-\alpha)=0
. The
O(\epsilon^{2})
terms give

\displaystyle(I-H_{0})\bar{\zeta}^{(2)}=

\displaystyle H_{1}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}=[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\bar{B}e^{-i\phi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i[\zeta^{(1)},I-H_{0}]\bar{B}e^{-i\phi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i(I-H_{0})|B|^{2}+O(\epsilon).

We pick

(5.16)

\zeta^{(2)}=\frac{i}{2}(I+H_{0})|B|^{2}+\frac{i}{2}M(|B|^{2}),

where
M(|B|^{2})=\frac{1}{2q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}|B(X,T)|^{2}\,d\alpha
.

5.3.3.
Expansion of
b

We expand
b
as

(5.17)

b=b^{(0)}+\epsilon b^{(1)}+\epsilon^{2}b^{(2)}+O(\epsilon^{3}).

Since
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})b=-[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}
is quadratic, we have
b^{(0)}=b^{(1)}=0
.
For
b_{2}
, one has

(5.18)

\begin{split}(I-H_{0})b^{(2)}=&-[\partial_{t_{0}}\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}\\
=&-\omega[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}=\omega[\zeta^{(1)},I-H_{0}]\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}=-\omega(I-H_{0})|B|^{2}.\end{split}

Since
b^{(2)}
is real, we get

(5.19)

b^{(2)}=-\omega|B|^{2}.

5.3.4.
Expansion of
A

We need also to expand
A=\sum_{n\geq 0}\epsilon^{n}A^{(n)}
.

Since
A-1
is quadratic by the formula (
3.9
), clearly,
A^{(0)}=1
, and
A^{(1)}=0
.

To find
A^{(2)}
, again from (
3.9
), at the
\epsilon^{2}
level, we have

\displaystyle(I-H_{0})A^{(2)}=i[\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}+i[\partial_{t_{0}}\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\partial_{t_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}=0.

Since
A^{(2)}
is real, we get
A^{(2)}=0
.

5.3.5.
Expansions of
G_{1}
and
G_{2}

From formula (
3.19
), by direct inspection, we obtain

(5.20)

G_{1}=O(\epsilon^{4}).

For
G_{2}
, integration by parts,

\displaystyle G_{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{3}}{4\pi q^{2}i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{\partial_{t_{0}}\zeta^{(1)}(\alpha,t)-\partial_{t_{0}}\zeta^{(1)}(\beta,t)}{\sin(\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2q})}\Big)^{2}\partial_{\beta_{0}}(\zeta^{(1)}-\bar{\zeta}^{(1)})\,d\beta+O(\epsilon^{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\epsilon^{3}[\partial_{t_{0}}\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}](\partial_{t_{0}}\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\zeta^{(1)}\partial_{\alpha_{0}}(\zeta^{(1)}-\bar{\zeta}^{(1)})-[\partial_{t_{0}}\zeta^{(1)},[\partial_{t_{0}}\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}^{2}(\zeta^{(1)}-\bar{\zeta}^{(1)})+O(\epsilon^{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\epsilon^{3}|B|^{2}Be^{i\phi}+O(\epsilon^{4}).

5.3.6.
O(\epsilon^{3})
Level

We first note that

(5.21)

(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})(I-H_{0})e^{i\phi}=2(1-\omega^{2})e^{i\phi}=2\left(1-\Big(1+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}+O(\epsilon)^{3}\Big)^{2}\right)e^{i\phi}=-2\epsilon^{2}e^{i\phi}.

At the
O(\epsilon^{3})
level of
(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})Be^{i\phi}
, we have
-2Be^{i\phi}
.
Now we expand the momentum equation, the first equation of (
3.1
), in term of powers of
\epsilon
as before. At the level of
O(\epsilon^{3})
, we have

(5.22)

\begin{split}&(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(3)}\\
=&-(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})(-H^{(1)})\zeta^{(2)}-(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})(-H^{(2)})\zeta^{(1)}\\
&-(2\partial_{t_{0}}\partial_{t_{1}}-i\partial_{\alpha_{1}})(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(2)}-(2\partial_{t_{0}\partial_{t_{1}}}-i\partial_{\alpha_{1}})(-H^{(1)})\zeta^{(1)}\\
&-(2\partial_{t_{0}t_{2}}+\partial_{t_{1}}^{2}+2b^{(2)}\partial_{t_{0}}\partial_{\alpha_{0}})(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(1)}+G_{2}+2Be^{i\phi}.\\
\end{split}

\bullet
Noticing that
\zeta^{(2)}
is slowly varying, one has

(5.23)

-(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})(-H^{(1)})\zeta^{(2)}=O(\epsilon).

\bullet
By our choice of
\zeta^{(2)}
, (
5.16
), and applying (3) of Lemma
2.2
, we have,

(5.24)

\zeta^{(2)}=\frac{i}{2}(I+H_{0})|B|^{2}+\frac{i}{2}M(|B|^{2})=\frac{i}{2}(I+H_{0})(|B|^{2}-M(|B|^{2}))+iM(|B|^{2}).

Applying (3) of Lemma
2.2
again, we obtain

(5.25)

\displaystyle(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(2)}=

\displaystyle(I-H_{0})\Big\{\frac{i}{2}(I+H_{0})\Big(|B|^{2}-M(|B|^{2})\Big)+iM(|B|^{2})\Big\}=iM(|B|^{2}).

Since
M(|B|^{2})
is slowly varying in
t
, we obtain

(5.26)

-(2\partial_{t_{0}}\partial_{t_{1}}-i\partial_{\alpha_{1}})(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(2)}=O(\epsilon).

Using Lemma
5.1
, we have

\displaystyle(-H^{(1)})\zeta^{(1)}=

\displaystyle-[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\zeta^{(1)}=O(\epsilon^{4}).

So we obtain

\displaystyle-(2\partial_{t_{0}}\partial_{t_{1}}-i\partial_{\alpha_{1}})(I-H_{0})(\zeta^{(2)}-\bar{\zeta}^{(2)})-(2\partial_{t_{0}\partial_{t_{1}}}-i\partial_{\alpha_{1}})(-H^{(1)})(\zeta^{(1)}-\bar{\zeta}^{(1)})=O(\epsilon).

\bullet
For
H^{(2)}\zeta^{(1)}
, by (
2.31
), Lemma
5.1
and the fact that
\zeta^{(2)}
is slowly varying, we obtain

\displaystyle H^{(2)}\zeta^{(1)}=

\displaystyle[\zeta^{(2)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\zeta^{(1)}-[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\zeta_{\alpha_{0}}^{(1)}\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\zeta^{(1)}+\frac{1}{2}[\zeta^{(1)},[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}^{2}\zeta^{(1)}=O(\epsilon^{4}).

\bullet
Also we have

(5.27)

\displaystyle-(2\partial_{t_{0}t_{2}}+\partial_{t_{1}}^{2}+2b^{(2)}\partial_{t_{0}}\partial_{\alpha_{0}})(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(1)}

(5.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Big\{2(i\omega)\partial_{T}+(\frac{1}{2\omega})^{2}\partial_{XX}+2(-\omega|B|^{2})(i\omega)(i)\Big\}Be^{i\phi}

(5.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Big\{2i\omega B_{T}+(\frac{1}{2\omega})^{2}B_{XX}+2\omega^{2}|B|^{2}B\Big\}e^{i\phi}

Overall, from all computations above, we obtain

(5.30)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(3)}=-2\Big\{2i\omega B_{T}+(\frac{1}{2\omega})^{2}B_{XX}+(2\omega^{2}-1)|B|^{2}B-B\Big\}e^{i\phi}.

Since
\omega=1+O(\epsilon^{2})
, we have

(5.31)

-2\Big\{2i\omega B_{T}+(\frac{1}{2\omega})^{2}B_{XX}+(2\omega^{2}-1)|B|^{2}B-B\Big\}e^{i\phi}=-2\Big\{2iB_{T}+\frac{1}{4}B_{XX}+|B|^{2}B-B\Big\}+O(\epsilon^{2}).

To avoid the secular growth, we choose
B
such that

(5.32)

2iB_{T}+\frac{1}{4}B_{XX}+|B|^{2}B-B=0.

or equivalently,

(5.33)

iB_{T}+\frac{1}{8}B_{XX}+\frac{1}{2}|B|^{2}B-\frac{1}{2}B=0.

Remark 5.1
.

Note that
B\equiv i
is an exact solution to (
5.33
), which justifies our assumption that

(5.34)

B=i+\text{perturbation}

in the long-wave perturbation setting.

With the choice of
B
above, (
5.22
) becomes

(\partial_{t_{0}}^{2}-i\partial_{\alpha_{0}})(I-H_{0})\zeta^{(3)}=O(\epsilon).

From
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\bar{\zeta}-\alpha)=0
, we have

\displaystyle(I-H_{0})\bar{\zeta}^{(3)}=

\displaystyle H^{(1)}\bar{\zeta}^{(2)}+H^{(2)}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(2)}+[\zeta^{(2)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}+[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{1}}\zeta^{(1)}

\displaystyle-[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\overline{\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\zeta^{(1)}}\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\zeta^{(1)}+\frac{1}{2}[\zeta^{(1)},[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}^{2}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}.

\bullet
Since
\bar{\zeta}^{(2)}
is slowly varying, we have

(5.35)

[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(2)}=O(\epsilon).

\bullet
For
[\zeta^{(2)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}
, since
\zeta^{(2)}
slowly varying, using Lemma
9.4
,

(5.36)

\displaystyle[\zeta^{(2)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}=[\zeta^{(2)},I+H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle O(\epsilon^{4}).

\bullet
For
[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{1}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}
, one has

(5.37)

\displaystyle[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{1}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\partial_{\alpha_{1}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}=(I-H_{0})BB_{X}.

\bullet
For
-[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\overline{\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\zeta^{(1)}}\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}
, it is easy to obtain

(5.38)

\displaystyle-[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\overline{\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\zeta^{(1)}}\partial_{\alpha_{0}}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}=O(\epsilon^{4}).

\bullet
For
\frac{1}{2}[\zeta^{(1)},[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}^{2}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}
, we have

(5.39)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[\zeta^{(1)},[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]]\partial_{\alpha_{0}}^{2}\bar{\zeta}^{(1)}=-\frac{1}{2}[\zeta^{(1)},[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]]\bar{B}e^{-i\phi}

(5.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\zeta^{(1)}[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]\bar{B}e^{-i\phi}+\frac{1}{2}[\zeta^{(1)},H_{0}]|B|^{2}

(5.41)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\zeta^{(1)}[\zeta^{(1)},I-H_{0}]\bar{B}e^{-i\phi}+\frac{1}{2}[\zeta^{(1)},I+H_{0}]|B|^{2}

(5.42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\zeta^{(1)}(I-H_{0})|B|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\zeta^{(1)}(I+H_{0})|B|^{2}

(5.43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|B|^{2}\zeta^{(1)}.

Therefore we conclude that

(5.44)

(I-H_{0})\bar{\zeta}^{(3)}=-B|B|^{2}e^{i\phi}+(I-H_{0})B\bar{B}_{X}

Now we can choose

(5.45)

\begin{split}\zeta^{(3)}=&-\frac{1}{2}\bar{B}|B|^{2}e^{-i\phi}+\frac{1}{2}(I+H_{0})(\bar{B}B_{X}).\end{split}

5.4.
The approximate solution

After choosing
\zeta^{(j)},\,j=1,2,3
above, with (
5.10
), (
5.3
), (
5.16
) and (
5.45
), we can define the approximate solution as

(5.46)

\zeta_{app}(\alpha,t)=\zeta_{ST}+(\alpha+\epsilon\zeta^{(1)}+\epsilon^{2}\zeta^{(2)}+\epsilon^{3}\zeta^{(3)}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}).

Explicitly plugging in the choices of
\zeta^{(j)}
, one has

(5.47)

\begin{split}\alpha+\epsilon\zeta^{(1)}+\epsilon^{2}\zeta^{(2)}+\epsilon^{3}\zeta^{(3)}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}=&\epsilon(B-i)e^{i\phi}+\epsilon^{2}\Big(\frac{ik}{2}(I+H_{0})|B|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}M(|B|^{2})-i\Big)\\
&+\epsilon^{3}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\bar{B}|B|^{2}e^{-i\phi}+\frac{1}{2}(I+H_{0})(\bar{B}B_{X})+\frac{i}{2}e^{-i\phi}\Big).\end{split}

Therefore with this choice of
\zeta_{app}
, we have

(5.48)

\left\lVert\zeta_{app}-\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}\left\lVert B-i\right\rVert_{H^{s+7}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})},

where
C>0
is a constant depending on
s
only.

5.5.
NLS estimates

In the final part of this section, we discuss the behavior of the function
B
coming from the expansion (
5.2
) and (
5.3
). From our multi-scale analysis,
B
solves

(5.49)

iB_{T}+\frac{1}{8}B_{XX}+\frac{1}{2}|B|^{2}B-\frac{1}{2}B=0

and the Stokes wave gives a special solution
B_{0}=i
. We perturb the special solution by considering solution of the form
B=i(1+\psi)
. We have the following result on its instability.

Proposition 5.1
.

For any given
0<\delta\ll 1
, there exist
\mu
satisfying
\left|\delta\right|\ll\mu<1

and
T_{0}=\log\left(\frac{\mu}{\delta}\right)
such that for any solution
B
to (
5.49
) with initial data

(5.50)

\left\lVert B(\cdot,0)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}=\delta

it satisfies the following growth estimate:

(5.51)

\left\|B(\cdot,t)-i\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\delta e^{t},\,\forall t\in\left[0,T_{0}\right].

Moreover, there exists solution
B
satisfying the initial condition (
5.50
), growth estimate and the the following unstable condition

(5.52)

\left\|B(\cdot,T_{0})-i\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}\geq\frac{1}{4}\mu\gg\delta.

Proof.

Making the change of variable
u(y,s)=e^{is}B(y/2,2s)
, then it solves

(5.53)

i\partial_{s}u+\partial^{2}_{y}u+|u|^{2}u=0

and the special solution given by the Stokes wave is
u_{0}(y,s)=ie^{is}
. Then applying Theorem
D.2
from Appendix
D
, the desired estimates follow.

∎

6.
The error equation

In this section, we derive governing equations for the remainder term. Let’s denote

(6.1)

\tilde{\zeta}:=\alpha+\epsilon\zeta^{(1)}+\epsilon^{2}\zeta^{(2)}+\epsilon^{3}\zeta^{(3)}.

Then

(6.2)

\zeta_{app}=\zeta_{ST}+(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}).

Define the error term as

(6.3)

r:=\zeta-\zeta_{app}.

6.1.
Notations

We first introduce some notations here.

Denote

(6.4)

\tilde{b}=\epsilon^{2}b^{(2)},\quad\tilde{b}_{ST}:=\epsilon^{2}b_{ST}^{(2)},

(6.5)

\tilde{A}:=1,\quad\quad\tilde{A}_{ST}:=1,

(6.6)

\tilde{D}_{t}:=\partial_{t}+\tilde{b}\partial_{\alpha},\quad\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}:=\partial_{t}+\tilde{b}_{ST}\partial_{\alpha},

(6.7)

\tilde{\theta}:=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})(\tilde{\zeta}-\alpha),\quad\tilde{\theta}_{ST}:=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}})(\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-\alpha),

(6.8)

\theta:=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\zeta-\alpha),\quad\theta_{ST}:=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})(\zeta_{ST}-\alpha),

(6.9)

\tilde{\mathcal{P}}=\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}-i\tilde{A}\partial_{\alpha},\quad\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST}=(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}-i\tilde{A}_{ST}\partial_{\alpha},

(6.10)

\begin{cases}\mathcal{Q}:=\mathcal{P}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}),\\
\mathcal{Q}_{ST}:=\mathcal{P}_{ST}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})\\
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}:=\tilde{\mathcal{P}}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})\\
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST}:=\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}).\end{cases}

With notations above, we immediately conclude the following:

Lemma 6.1
.

We have

(6.11)

\left\lVert\tilde{\mathcal{P}}\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST}\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\|B-i\|_{H^{s+7}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

6.2.
Governing equation for
r

From the cubic structure for
\zeta
, (
3.19
), we have

(6.12)

\mathcal{P}\theta=G.

And similarly, one has

(6.13)

\mathcal{P}_{ST}\theta_{ST}=G_{ST}.

Consider the quantity
\rho
defined by

(6.14)

\rho:=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\Big[\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST})\Big].

Then
\rho
is holomorphic in
\Omega(t)^{c}
. In Lemma
7.5
, we will show that
\rho
is equivalent to
r
.

To control
\rho
and therefore control
r
, we need to derive a nice structure for
\rho
. To achieve the nonlinear instability, it is necessary to obtain the control of
\rho
for
t\in[0,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]
, where
\delta:=\|B(\cdot,0)-i\|_{H^{s+7}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}
. So we need to derive an equation for
\rho
of the form

(6.15)

\mathcal{P}\rho=O(\epsilon^{2})O(\rho)

such that

(6.16)

\|D_{t}\rho(\cdot,t)\|_{H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\sim\delta\epsilon^{3/2}.

By the definition of
\rho
, using the notation above, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\rho=

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\theta-\mathcal{P}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\theta_{ST}-\mathcal{P}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}\theta-\mathcal{Q}\theta_{ST}-\mathcal{Q}(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}\theta-\mathcal{Q}_{ST}\theta_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST})\theta_{ST}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\tilde{\theta}-(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})\tilde{\theta}+\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST}\tilde{\theta}_{ST}+(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST})\tilde{\theta}_{St}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G-G_{ST}-(\tilde{G}-\tilde{G}_{ST})+\Big\{-(\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST})\theta_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})\tilde{\theta}+(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST})\tilde{\theta}_{St}\Big\}.

We regroup
\Big\{-(\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST})\theta_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})\tilde{\theta}+(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\Big\}
as

\displaystyle(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST})\theta_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})\tilde{\theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\Big\}+\Big\{(\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST})(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST})\Big\}-(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})\tilde{\theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{Q}_{ST}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})\tilde{\theta}+\Big\{(\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST})(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST})\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{Q}_{ST}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})\Big)\tilde{\theta}_{ST}+(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\tilde{\theta})+\Big\{(\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST})(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST})\Big\}

To sum up, we obtain

(6.17)

\mathcal{P}\rho=N_{1}+N_{2}+N_{3}+N_{4},

where

(6.18)

N_{1}=G-G_{ST}-(\tilde{G}-\tilde{G}_{ST}),

(6.19)

N_{2}=\Big(\mathcal{Q}_{ST}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})\Big)\tilde{\theta}_{ST},

(6.20)

N_{3}=(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\tilde{\theta}),

(6.21)

N_{4}=(\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST})(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST}).

6.3.
Equation governing
D_{t}\rho

We next derive an equation for
D_{t}\rho
. Define

(6.22)

\sigma:=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})D_{t}\rho.

Applying
\mathcal{P}
and using the equation for
\rho
obtained above, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\sigma=

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}D_{t}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\rho+\mathcal{P}[D_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{t}\mathcal{P}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\rho+[\mathcal{P},D_{t}]\rho+\mathcal{P}[D_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{t}N_{1}+D_{t}N_{2}+D_{t}N_{3}+D_{t}N_{4}+[\mathcal{P},D_{t}]\rho+\mathcal{P}[D_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\rho

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle M_{1}+M_{2}+M_{3}+M_{4}+M_{5}+M_{6}.

6.4.
Energy functional

Lemma 6.2

(Basic lemma, Lemma 4.1 in
[
74
]
)
.

Let
\Theta
satisfy the equation

(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\Theta=G.

Suppose that
\Theta,D_{t}\Theta,G\in H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})
for some
s\geq 4
. Define

(6.23)

E_{0}(t):=\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{1}{A}|D_{t}\Theta(\alpha,t)|^{2}+i\Theta(\alpha,t)\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\Theta}(\alpha,t)\,d\alpha.

Then

(6.24)

\frac{dE_{0}}{dt}=\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{2}{A}\Re(D_{t}\Theta\bar{G})-\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\frac{1}{A}|D_{t}\Theta|^{2}\,d\alpha.

Moreover, if
\Theta
is the boundary value of a holomorphic function in
\Omega(t)^{c}
, then

(6.25)

\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}i\Theta\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\Theta}d\alpha=-\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}i\bar{\Theta}\partial_{\alpha}\Theta\,d\alpha\geq 0.

Notations:
Denote

(6.26)

\rho^{(n)}:=\partial_{\alpha}^{n}\rho,\quad\quad\quad\sigma^{(n)}:=\partial_{\alpha}^{n}\sigma.

Because
\rho^{(n)}
and
\sigma^{(n)}
are not necessarily holomorphic in
\Omega(t)^{c}
, we decompose them as

(6.27)

\begin{split}\rho^{n}=&\frac{1}{2}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\rho^{n}+\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\rho^{(n)}:=\phi^{(n)}+\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\\
\sigma^{(n)}=&\frac{1}{2}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\sigma^{(n)}+\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\sigma^{n}:=\Psi^{(n)}+\mathcal{S}^{(n)}.\end{split}

and define

(6.28)

\mathcal{E}_{n}(t):=\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{1}{A}|D_{t}\rho^{(n)}|^{2}+i\phi^{(n)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\phi}^{(n)}\,d\alpha.

(6.29)

\mathcal{F}_{n}(t):=\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{1}{A}|D_{t}\sigma^{(n)}|^{2}+i\sigma^{(n)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\sigma}^{(n)}\,d\alpha.

Define the energy as

(6.30)

\mathcal{E}(t):=\sum_{n=0}^{s}(\mathcal{E}_{n}(t)+\mathcal{F}_{n}(t)).

By lemma
6.2
, each
\mathcal{E}_{n}
is positive.

6.5.
Evolution of
\mathcal{E}_{n}
and
\mathcal{F}_{n}

To show that
r
remains small (in the sense of some appropriate norm), we need to show that the energy
\mathcal{E}
remains small for a long time. To achieve this goal, we analyze the evolution of
\mathcal{E}_{n}
and
\mathcal{F}_{n}
. Note that

(6.31)

\begin{split}(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\rho^{(n)}=&\partial_{\alpha}^{n}(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\rho+[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\rho\\
=&\sum_{m=1}^{4}\partial_{\alpha}^{n}N_{m}+[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\rho\\
:=&\mathcal{C}_{1,n}.\end{split}

Similarly, we derive the governing equation for
\sigma^{(n)}=\partial_{\alpha}^{n}\sigma
. By direct computations, one has

(6.32)

\begin{split}(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\sigma^{(n)}=&\partial_{\alpha}^{n}(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\sigma+[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\sigma\\
:=&C_{2,n}.\end{split}

By the basic lemma
6.2
, equations (
6.31
) and (
6.32
), we have

(6.33)

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{E}_{n}(t)=&\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{2}{A}\Re(D_{t}\rho^{(n)}\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{1,n})-\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\frac{1}{A}|D_{t}\rho^{(n)}|^{2}\,d\alpha\\
&+2\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\phi}^{(n)}+\partial_{t}\mathcal{\phi}^{(n)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{(n)}+\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{(n)}\,d\alpha\end{split}

and

(6.34)

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{n}(t)=&\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{2}{A}\Re(D_{t}\sigma^{(n)}\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{2,n})-\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\frac{1}{A}|D_{t}\sigma^{(n)}|^{2}\,d\alpha.\end{split}

7.
Preparations for energy estimates

In this section, we estimate the quantities which will be used in the energy estimates in the next section. We bound these quantities in terms of an auxiliary quantity
E_{s}
, which is essentially equivalent to the energy
\mathcal{E}
.

7.1.
An auxiliary quantity for the energy functional and a priori assumptions

The energy functional
\mathcal{E}
is not very convenient in the energy estimates, so we introduce the quantity

(7.1)

E_{s}(t)^{1/2}:=\left\lVert D_{t}r(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert r_{\alpha}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}r(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

Let
T_{0}>0
. We make the following a priori assumptions.

1.

(Bootstrap assumption 1)

(7.2)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}E_{s}(t)^{1/2}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},\quad\quad\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon q^{1/2}.

2.

(Assumption 2) We assume
B
satisfies

(7.3)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert B(\epsilon(\alpha+\frac{1}{2\omega}t),\epsilon^{2}t)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{-1/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Here,
C>0
is a constant depending on
s
only.

Remark 7.1
.

The bootstrap assumption (
7.2
) will be justified easily by the energy estimates in §
8
. The assumption (
7.3
) is satisfied for
B(\alpha,t)
given in Proposition
5.1
.

We will control
\frac{d\mathcal{E}}{dt}
in terms of
E_{s}
and
\epsilon
. Then we can obtain energy estimates on a lifespan of length
O(\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta})
. For this purpose, we control the quantities appear in the energy estimates in terms of
E_{s}
and
\epsilon
.

Convention.
In this and the next sections, if not specified, we assume

0\leq t\leq\min\{T_{0},\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}\}

and the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
) holds. Here
0<\delta\ll 1
is an arbitrary given number and
\delta\ll\mu<1
is fixed, independent of
\delta
and
\epsilon
. These
\delta
and
\mu
are given as in Proposition
5.1
. If not specified,
C>0
is a constant depending on
s
only.

7.1.1.
Consequences of the a priori assumptions

Lemma 7.1
.

Assume (
7.3
), then we have

(7.4)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the definitions of
\tilde{\zeta}
and
\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}
and the assumption (
7.3
).
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

Assuming the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
), we have

1.

(7.5)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\|\zeta_{\alpha}(\cdot,t)-1\|_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon.

2.

(7.6)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta(\cdot,t)-\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,t))\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

3.

(7.7)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{1/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

4.

(7.8)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Proof.

We decompose
\zeta_{\alpha}-1
as

\displaystyle\zeta_{\alpha}-1=r_{\alpha}+\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST})+\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}-1.

Taking the norm, we get

(7.9)

\left\lVert\zeta_{\alpha}-1\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\leq\left\lVert r_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST})+\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}-1\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon.

where we applied the bootstrap assumption for the first term and used the definitions for
\tilde{\zeta}
and
\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}
, see (
6.1
) and (
5.10
) for the second term.

To estimate (
7.6
), we notice that

(7.10)

\zeta-\zeta_{ST}=r+(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}).

by the definition of
r
, (
6.3
).
By Lemma
7.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta-\zeta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert r_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

For (
7.7
), using

(7.11)

\begin{split}D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}=&D_{t}(\zeta-\zeta_{ST}-(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}))+D_{t}(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST})+(D_{t}-D_{t}^{ST})\zeta_{ST}\\
=&D_{t}r+(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST})+D_{t}^{ST}(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST})+(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}\end{split}

one has

\displaystyle\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert D_{t}r\right\rVert_{H^{s}}+C\epsilon^{1/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+C\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+C\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

Combing (
7.11
) together with

(7.12)

\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}=\tilde{D}_{t}(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST})+(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\tilde{\zeta}_{ST},

we obtain

(7.13)

\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

We are done.
∎

Remark 7.2
.

Using (
7.69
), one has

\displaystyle\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

So the bootstrap assumption
\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon q^{1/2}
can be easily justified once we establish the estimate
\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Assuming the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
), we have

(7.14)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert(I\pm\mathcal{K}_{\zeta}(\cdot,t))^{-1}f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})},\quad\quad\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert(I\pm\mathcal{K}_{\zeta_{ST}}(\cdot,t))^{-1}f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})},

and

(7.15)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert(I\pm\mathcal{K}_{\zeta}^{\ast}(\cdot,t))^{-1}f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})},\quad\quad\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert(I\pm\mathcal{K}_{\zeta_{ST}}^{\ast}(\cdot,t))^{-1}f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})},

where
\mathcal{K}_{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\gamma}
are the double layer potential operator and its adjoint associated with
\gamma
, see Definition
2.5
.

Lemma
7.3
is the direct consequence of Lemma
2.4
and the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
). Alternatively, we can also prove this lemma as follows.

Proof.

Let
f
be a real-valued function, then

(7.16)

\mathcal{K}_{\zeta}f(\alpha,t)=\Re\Big\{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}f(\alpha,t)-H_{0}f(\alpha,t)\Big\}.

The bootstrap assumption (
7.2
) and the Sobolev embedding Lemma
2.1
give us

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{K}_{\zeta}f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle C\left\lVert\zeta_{\alpha}-1\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\left\lVert\zeta_{\alpha}-1\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

So we have

(7.17)

\left\lVert\mathcal{K}_{\zeta}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{L}_{H^{s}\rightarrow H^{s}}}\leq C\epsilon,

which implies

(7.18)

\left\lVert(I\pm\mathcal{K}_{\zeta})^{-1}f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq(1+C\epsilon)\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})},

provided that
C\epsilon<1
and
t\leq\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}
. Other inequalities can be proved similarly.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Assume the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
). Let
g,h
be real functions. Suppose

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})h\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}=g\quad\quad\text{or}\quad\quad(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})h=g.

Then we have for any
t\in[0,T_{0}]
,

(7.19)

\|h\|_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\|g\|_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

Proof.

We consider the case
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})h=g
only, the case for
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})h\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}
follows in a similar manner. Since
h
is real, taking the real parts on both sides of
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})h=g
, we obtain

(7.20)

(I-\mathcal{K}_{\zeta})h=\Re\{g\}.

By Lemma
7.3
, we get

(7.21)

\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

as desired.
∎

7.1.2.
The equivalence of
\rho
and
r

Lemma 7.5
.

Assume the a priori assumption (
7.2
). We have

(7.22)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\rho-2r)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}\epsilon^{5/2},

(7.23)

\left\lVert D_{t}\rho-2D_{t}r\right\rVert_{H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C(\epsilon E_{s}^{1/2}+\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}\epsilon^{5/2})\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

(7.24)

\left\lVert D_{t}(D_{t}\rho-2D_{t}r)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma 8.3 in
[
62
]
. However, we need an additional gain of the factor
\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}
. For the sake of completeness, we provide the proof in Appendix
C
.

Corollary 7.1
.

Assuming the a priori assumption (
7.2
), we have

(7.25)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},\quad\quad\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s+1/2}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

7.2.
Bound
\tilde{b}
,
b
,
b_{ST}
,
\tilde{b}_{ST}
,
b-\tilde{b}
,
b_{ST}-\tilde{b}_{ST}
and
b-\tilde{b}-(b_{ST}-\tilde{b}_{ST})

7.2.1.
Estimate
\tilde{b}_{ST}

Recalling the notation (
6.4
), from Proposition
4.4
, we know that

\tilde{b}_{ST}=-\epsilon^{2}\omega.

7.2.2.
Estimate
b_{ST}

Recall that
b_{ST}
is given by

(7.26)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})b_{ST}=-[(D_{t}^{ST})\zeta_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}.

Also recall that
b_{ST}(\alpha,t)=b_{ST}(\alpha+\omega t,0)
since the Stokes wave is a traveling wave. Also see Corollary
4.2
. Since
b_{ST}
is real, by Lemma
7.4
and Proposition
2.1
, we have

(7.27)

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left\lVert b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq&C\left\lVert[(D_{t}^{ST})\zeta_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\left\lVert(D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)\right\rVert_{W^{s^{\prime}-1,\infty}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)-1\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
&+C\left\lVert(D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)\right\rVert_{W^{s^{\prime}-1,\infty}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)-1\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{2}q^{1/2}.\end{split}

By the Sobolev embedding, Lemma
2.1
, we have

(7.28)

\left\lVert b_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s^{\prime}-1}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

7.2.3.
Estimate
b_{ST}-\tilde{b}_{ST}

By direct computations, one has

(7.29)

\left\lVert b_{ST}-\tilde{b}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3}q^{1/2}.

Applying Lemma
2.1
, we obtain

(7.30)

\left\lVert b_{ST}-\tilde{b}_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s^{\prime}-1}}\leq C\epsilon^{3}.

7.2.4.
Estimate
\tilde{b}

Recall that
\tilde{b}=-\epsilon^{2}\omega|B|^{2}
, see (
5.19
). Here,
B=B(X,T)
. By the assumption (
7.3
), one has

(7.31)

\left\lVert\tilde{b}\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert|B|^{2}\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}q^{1/2}.

Using the Sobolev embedding again, Lemma
2.1
, it follows

(7.32)

\left\lVert\tilde{b}\right\rVert_{W^{s^{\prime}-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

Moreover, we also obtain

(7.33)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

7.2.5.
Estimate
b
and
b-b_{ST}

Recall that

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})b=-[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}.

Also,

(7.34)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})b_{ST}=-[D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}.

Taking the difference of two expressions above, after some simple manipulations, we get

(7.35)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})(b-b_{ST})=(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})b_{ST}-\Big([D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-[D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}\Big).

By (3) of Lemma
2.3
, (
7.6
), and (
7.27
), we have

(7.36)

\left\lVert(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta-\zeta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert b_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Applying (
2.40
) of Proposition
2.2
with
\zeta_{1}:=\zeta
,
\zeta_{2}:=\zeta_{ST}
,
f_{1}:=D_{t}\zeta
,
f_{2}:=D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}
,
g_{1}:=\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}
, and
g_{2}:=\bar{\zeta}_{ST}
, together with (
7.2
) and (
7.3
), one has

\displaystyle\left\lVert[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-[D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta-\zeta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\left\lVert\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}+C\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta-\zeta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

From computations above, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})},

which implies

(7.37)

\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

As a consequence, we obtain

(7.38)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}q^{1/2}+C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}\leq C\epsilon^{2}q^{1/2},

(7.39)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

(7.40)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}q^{-1/2}.

7.2.6.
Estimate
b-b_{ST}-(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})

Note that by the explicit formula of
\tilde{b}
and
\tilde{b}_{ST}
,

(7.41)

\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST}=-[\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}-1}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\alpha}}+[\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}+e,

where

(7.42)

\left\lVert e\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Applying Proposition
2.4
with
\zeta_{1}:=\zeta
,
\zeta_{2}:=\zeta_{ST}
,
\zeta_{3}:=\tilde{\zeta}
,
\zeta_{4}:=\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}
,
g_{1}:=D_{t}\zeta
,
g_{2}:=D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}
,
g_{3}:=\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}
,
g_{4}:=\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}
,
f_{1}:=\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}-1
,
f_{2}:=\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1
,
f_{3}:=\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}-1
,
f_{4}:=\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-1
, from Proposition
2.2
and Proposition
2.4

, Proposition
2.3
, Lemma
7.2
, it follows

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Big([D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-[D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}\Big)-\Big([\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}-1}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\alpha}}-[\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}\Big)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{D}_{t}\zeta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\left\lVert\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{D}_{t}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Therefore, we obtain the following results:

(7.43)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert b-b_{ST}-(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

(7.44)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}\left\lVert b-b_{ST}-(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}q^{-1/2}.

7.3.
Bound
\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}
,
D_{t}b
,
D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}
,
D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST}
,
D_{t}b-\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}
,
D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})

7.3.1.
Estimate
\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}

Since
\tilde{b}=-\epsilon^{2}\omega|B|^{2}
,
\tilde{D}_{t}=\partial_{t}+\tilde{b}\partial_{\alpha}
, and

\partial_{t}B=\frac{\epsilon}{2\omega}B_{X}+\epsilon^{2}B_{T},\partial_{\alpha}B=\epsilon B_{X},

we directly obtain

(7.45)

\left\lVert\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}\epsilon^{5/2},

and

(7.46)

\left\lVert\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}\epsilon^{5/2}q^{-1/2}.

7.3.2.
Estimate
\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST}
,
D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}
and
D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST}

We simply have

(7.47)

\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST}=0

due to
\tilde{b}_{ST}=-\omega
. Since

(7.48)

D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}=\partial_{t}b_{ST}+b_{ST}\partial_{\alpha}b_{ST}

and
b_{ST}(\alpha,t)=b_{ST}(\alpha+\omega t)
, we have (noting that
b_{ST}=-\omega\epsilon^{2}+O(\epsilon^{3})
)

(7.49)

\left\lVert D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3}q^{1/2}.

Therefore,

(7.50)

\left\lVert D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3}q^{1/2}.

Corollary 7.2
.

Assuming the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
), we have

(7.51)

\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\zeta-(D_{t}^{ST})^{2}\zeta_{ST}-\Big(\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}\tilde{\zeta}-(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}\Big)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+\left\lVert D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

Proof.

Writing
\zeta=r+\zeta_{ST}+\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}
, one has

\displaystyle D_{t}^{2}\zeta-(D_{t}^{ST})^{2}\zeta_{ST}-\Big(\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}\tilde{\zeta}-(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{t}^{2}r+\Big(D_{t}^{2}-(D_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)\zeta_{ST}+\Big(D_{t}^{2}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}\Big)\tilde{\zeta}-\Big(D_{t}^{2}-(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{t}^{2}r+\Big(D_{t}^{2}-(D_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)\zeta_{ST}+\Big(D_{t}^{2}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}-\Big(D_{t}^{2}-(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)\Big)\tilde{\zeta}-\Big(D_{t}^{2}-(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)(\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-\tilde{\zeta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{t}^{2}r+\Big(D_{t}^{2}-(D_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)\zeta_{ST}-\Big(\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}-(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)\tilde{\zeta}-\Big(D_{t}^{2}-(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)(\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-\tilde{\zeta})

Using

(7.52)

\begin{split}D_{t}^{2}-(D_{t}^{ST})^{2}=&D_{t}(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}+(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{t}+(b-b_{ST})b\partial_{\alpha}^{2}+(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{ST},\end{split}

one obtains

(7.53)

\left\lVert(D_{t}^{2}-(D_{t}^{ST})^{2})\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

By the similar algebraic manipulations, one has

(7.54)

\left\lVert\Big(\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}-(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)\tilde{\zeta}+\Big(D_{t}^{2}-(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\Big)(\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-\tilde{\zeta})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

So we conclude the proof of the corollary.
∎

7.3.3.
Estimate
D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})

Applying
D_{t}
on both sides of
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})b=-[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1)}{\zeta_{\alpha}},

we obtain (for the derivation in the Euclidean setting, see Proposition 2.7 of
[
74
]
.)

(7.55)

\begin{split}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})D_{t}b=&[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(2b-D_{t}\bar{\zeta})}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-[D_{t}^{2}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\\
&+\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}{2q})}\Big)^{2}(\bar{\zeta}_{\beta}(\beta)-1)\,d\beta.\end{split}

Similarly, we have

(7.56)

\begin{split}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}=&[D_{t}^{ST}\zeta^{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(2b_{ST}-D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST})}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}-[(D_{t}^{ST})^{2}\zeta_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}\\
&+\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\alpha)-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\beta)}{\sin(\frac{\zeta_{ST}(\alpha)-\zeta_{ST}(\beta)}{2q})}\Big)^{2}(\partial_{\beta}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1)\,d\beta.\end{split}

Taking the difference of two expressions above, we obtain

\displaystyle(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}+\Big\{[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(2b-D_{t}\bar{\zeta})}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-[D_{t}^{ST}\zeta^{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(2b_{ST}-D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST})}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}\Big\}

\displaystyle-\Big\{[D_{t}^{2}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-[(D_{t}^{ST})^{2}\zeta_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}\Big\}

\displaystyle+\Big\{\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}{2q})}\Big)^{2}(\bar{\zeta}_{\beta}(\beta)-1)\,d\beta

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\alpha)-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\beta)}{\sin(\frac{\zeta_{ST}(\alpha)-\zeta_{ST}(\beta)}{2q})}\Big)^{2}(\partial_{\beta}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1)\,d\beta\Big\}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle(I-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}})\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST}+\Big\{[\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(2\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}\bar{\tilde{\zeta}})}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\alpha}}-[\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}^{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(2\tilde{b}_{ST}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{ST})}{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}\Big\}

\displaystyle-\Big\{[\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}\tilde{\zeta},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}]\frac{\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{\alpha}-1}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\alpha}}-[(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}\Big\}

\displaystyle+\Big\{\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}(\alpha)-\tilde{D}_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\sin(\frac{\tilde{\zeta}(\alpha)-\tilde{\zeta}(\beta)}{2q})}\Big)^{2}(\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{\beta}(\beta)-1)\,d\beta

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\alpha)-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\beta)}{\sin(\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha)-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}(\beta)}{2q})}\Big)^{2}(\partial_{\beta}\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{ST}-1)\,d\beta\Big\}+e,

where

(7.57)

\left\lVert e\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Applying (3) of Lemma
2.3
, one has

(7.58)

\left\lVert(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta-\zeta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Similarly,

(7.59)

\left\lVert(\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}})\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Using the same argument as for estimating
\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}
, together with the estimates (
7.7
), (
7.37
), one has

(7.60)

\begin{split}\left\lVert[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(2b)}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-[D_{t}^{ST}\zeta^{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(2b_{ST})}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.\end{split}

Similarly,

(7.61)

\left\lVert[\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(2\tilde{b})}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\alpha}}-[\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}^{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(2\tilde{b}_{ST})}{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Taking the differences and using Proposition
2.1
, we obtain

(7.62)

\begin{split}&\Big|\Big|\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)}\Big)^{2}(\bar{\zeta}_{\beta}(\beta)-1)\,d\beta\\
&-\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\alpha)-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\beta)}{\zeta_{ST}(\alpha)-\zeta_{ST}(\beta)}\Big)^{2}(\partial_{\beta}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1)\,d\beta\Big|\Big|_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\Big(\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}+\left\lVert D_{t}^{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\Big)\Big(\left\lVert\zeta_{\alpha}-1\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}-1\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\Big)\\
&+\Big(\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}^{2}+\left\lVert D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\Big)^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta-\zeta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.\end{split}

Similarly,

(7.63)

\begin{split}&\Big|\Big|\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}(\alpha)-\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}(\beta)}{\tilde{\zeta}(\alpha)-\tilde{\zeta}(\beta)}\Big)^{2}(\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{\beta}(\beta)-1)\,d\beta\\
&-\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha)-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}(\beta)}{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha)-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}(\beta)}\Big)^{2}(\partial_{\beta}\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{ST}-1)\,d\beta\Big|\Big|_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.\end{split}

Applying Proposition
2.4
, Lemma
7.2
, (
7.37
), we have

(7.64)

\begin{split}&\Big|\Big|[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-[D_{t}^{ST}\zeta^{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}\\
&-\Big([\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{D}_{t}\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\alpha}}-[\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}^{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{ST}}{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}\Big)\Big|\Big|_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\epsilon\left\lVert D_{t}\zeta-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{S}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}r\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.\end{split}

By Proposition
2.4
and using Corollary
7.2
, we obtain

(7.65)

\begin{split}&\Big|\Big|[D_{t}^{2}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}-1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-[(D_{t}^{ST})^{2}\zeta_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}}\\
&-\Big\{[\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}\tilde{\zeta},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}]\frac{\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{\alpha}-1}{\tilde{\zeta}_{\alpha}}-[(\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST})^{2}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{ST}-1}{\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}\Big\}\Big|\Big|\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon\left\lVert D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.\end{split}

So we obtain

\begin{split}&\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST})-\Big\{(I-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})\Big\}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon\left\lVert D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})},\end{split}

which implies

\displaystyle\left\lVert(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon\left\lVert D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle+C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Here, we used the fact

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Therefore, we can use Lemma
7.4
to conclude

(7.66)

\left\lVert D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Since
\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST}=0
, and

(7.67)

\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}=-\epsilon^{2}\omega k(\partial_{t}+b^{(2)}\partial_{\alpha})|B(X,T)|^{2},

(
7.66
) implies

(7.68)

\left\lVert D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Since
D_{t}(b-b_{ST})=D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}+(b_{ST}-b)\partial_{\alpha}b_{ST}
, we also conclude

(7.69)

\left\lVert D_{t}(b-b_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Using the same argument, we can also conclude

(7.70)

\left\lVert(D_{t}(b-b_{ST})-\tilde{D}_{t}(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

7.4.
Bounds for
A,\,A_{ST},\,\tilde{A},\,\tilde{A}_{ST}

First of all, by construction, one has
\tilde{A}=\tilde{A}_{ST}=1
.
Using the same arguments as for
b
,
b_{ST}
, respectively, we obtain

(7.71)

\left\|A-1\right\|_{H^{s}\left(q\mathbb{T}\right)}\leq C\epsilon^{3}q^{\frac{1}{2}}

which also implies

(7.72)

\left\|A-1\right\|_{W^{s-1,\infty}\left(q\mathbb{T}\right)}\leq C\epsilon^{3}

by the Sobolev embedding. As a consequence, for
t\in[0,T_{0}]
,

(7.73)

\inf_{\alpha\in q\mathbb{T})}A\geq\frac{1}{2}.

(7.74)

\left\|A_{ST}-1\right\|_{H^{s}\left(q\mathbb{T}\right)}\leq C\epsilon^{3}q^{\frac{1}{2}}

and

(7.75)

\left\|A_{ST}-1\right\|_{W^{s-1,\infty}\left(q\mathbb{T}\right)}\leq C\epsilon^{3}.

Using the same argument as for

D_{t}b-D_{t}^{ST}b_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{b}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{b}_{ST})
, we can conclude
that

(7.76)

\left\|A-A_{ST}\right\|_{H^{s}\left(q\mathbb{T}\right)}\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{5}{2}}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}

which also implies

(7.77)

\left\|A-A_{ST}\right\|_{W^{s-1,\infty}\left(q\mathbb{T}\right)}\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{5}{2}}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}

by the Sobolev embedding.

7.5.
Estimate sums of the form
\sum_{j=1}^{4}(-1)^{j-1}G_{\zeta_{j}}(f_{j},g_{j},h_{j})

Define

(7.78)

G_{\zeta}(g,h,f):=\frac{1}{4\pi q^{2}i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))(h(\alpha)-h(\beta))}{\Big(\sin(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)))\Big)^{2}}f_{\beta}\,d\beta

The expression defined above can be regarded as an alternating sums of quadrilinear form in terms of
(\zeta,g,h,f)
. In the remaining part, we estimates the differences of
G_{\zeta}(g,h,f)
associated with different quadruples.

In the view of our bootstrap assumptions, we assume that

(H1)

For all
t\in[0,T_{0}]
,
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
,

(7.79)

\left\lVert f_{j}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert g_{j}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert h_{j}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{j}-1\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon.

(H2)

For
j=1,2
,

(7.80)

\begin{split}\left\lVert f_{j}-f_{j+2}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}&+\left\lVert g_{j}-g_{j+2}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}+\left\lVert h_{j}-h_{j+2}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\\
&+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{j}-\zeta_{j+2})\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}.\end{split}

(H3)

(7.81)

\begin{split}\left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^{4}(-1)^{j-1}f_{j}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}&+\left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^{4}(-1)^{j-1}g_{j}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^{4}(-1)^{j-1}h_{j}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
&+\left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^{4}(-1)^{j-1}\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{j}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.\end{split}

Our goal is to estimate
\left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^{4}(-1)^{j-1}G_{\zeta_{j}}(g_{j},h_{j},f_{j})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}
.

Lemma 7.6
.

Under the assumptions (H1)-(H2)-(H3), we have

(7.82)

\left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^{4}(-1)^{j-1}G_{\zeta_{j}}(g_{j},h_{j},f_{j})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

The proof is provided in Appendix §
C.4
. We will apply Lemma
7.6
with
\zeta_{1}:=\zeta
,
\zeta_{2}:=\zeta_{ST}
,
\zeta_{3}:=\tilde{\zeta}
,
\zeta_{4}:=\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}
.
f_{j}
,
g_{j}
and
h_{j}
are quantities associated with
\zeta_{j}
. For example, we take
f_{1}=D_{t}\zeta
,
f_{2}=D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}
,
f_{3}=\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta}
,
f_{4}=\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}
. According to the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
) and the assumption (
7.3
),
\{\zeta_{j}\},\{f_{j}\},\{g_{j}\},\{h_{j}\}
satisfy (H1)-(H2)-(H3).

7.6.
Estimate
N_{1}:=G-G_{ST}-(\tilde{G}-\tilde{G}_{ST})

We can write
G=G_{1}+G_{2}
, where

(7.83)

G_{1}=-2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}\frac{1}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\zeta}\frac{1}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}]\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\zeta,

and

(7.84)

G_{2}=\frac{1}{4\pi q^{2}i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\sin(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)))}\Big)^{2}\partial_{\beta}(\zeta-\bar{\zeta})\,d\beta.

The terms
G_{j,ST}
,
\tilde{G}_{j}
, and
\tilde{G}_{j,ST}
are defined similarly.
Applying Lemma
7.6
with

(7.85)

\zeta_{1}:=\zeta\quad\quad\zeta_{2}:=\zeta_{ST},\quad\quad\zeta_{3}:=\tilde{\zeta},\quad\quad\zeta_{4}:=\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}

(7.86)

g_{1}=h_{1}:=D_{t}\zeta,\quad g_{2}=h_{2}:=D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST},\quad g_{3}=h_{3}:=\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{\zeta},\quad g_{4}=h_{4}:=\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{\zeta}_{ST},

(7.87)

f_{1}:=\zeta-\bar{\zeta},\quad f_{2}:=\zeta_{ST}-\bar{\zeta}_{ST},\quad f_{3}:=\tilde{\zeta}-\bar{\tilde{\zeta}},\quad f_{4}:=\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-\bar{\tilde{\zeta}}_{ST},

(7.88)

G_{\zeta_{1}}:=G_{2},\quad\quad G_{\zeta_{2}}:=G_{2,ST},\quad\quad G_{3}:=\tilde{G}_{2},\quad\quad G_{4}:=\tilde{G}_{2,ST}

one has

\displaystyle\left\lVert G_{2}-G_{2,ST}-(\tilde{G}_{2}-\tilde{G}_{2,ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{2}E_{s}^{1/2}+C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

We rewrite
G_{1}
as

(7.89)

\begin{split}G_{1}=&-\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{1}{q\pi i}\frac{\cos(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}+\overline{\frac{1}{q\pi i}\frac{\cos(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}}\Big)(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t))\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)\,d\beta\\
=&-\frac{2}{q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Im\Big\{\frac{\cos(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}\Big\}(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t))\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)\,d\beta\\
=&-\frac{2}{q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{\Im\Big\{\cos(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})\overline{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}\Big\}}{\Big|\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})\Big|^{2}}(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t))\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)\,d\beta\end{split}

Note that

(7.90)

\overline{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}=\sin(\frac{\bar{\zeta}(\alpha,t)-\bar{\zeta}(\beta,t)}{2q}).

Denoting
L_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta):=\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q}
, we have

(7.91)

\begin{split}&\cos(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})\sin(\frac{\bar{\zeta}(\alpha,t)-\bar{\zeta}(\beta,t)}{2q})\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sin(L_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta)+\bar{L}_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta))-\sin(L_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta)-\bar{L}_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta))\Big)\end{split}

With the computations above,
G_{1}
can be written as

(7.92)

\begin{split}G_{1}=&-\frac{1}{q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{\Im\Big\{\sin(L_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta)+\bar{L}_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta))-\sin(L_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta)-\bar{L}_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta))\Big\}}{\Big|\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})\Big|^{2}}\\
&\times(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t))\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)\,d\beta\end{split}

Noticing that

(7.93)

\Im\Big\{\sin(L_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta)+\bar{L}_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta))-\sin(L_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta)-\bar{L}_{\zeta}(\alpha,\beta))\Big\}\sim\Im\{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)\},

from which we see that
G_{1}
is a cubic term. Moreover, using the same argument as for
G_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert G_{1}-G_{1,ST}-(\tilde{G}_{1}-\tilde{G}_{1,ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{2}E_{s}^{1/2}+C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

So we conclude that

(7.94)

\left\lVert G-G_{ST}-(\tilde{G}-\tilde{G}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}E_{s}^{1/2}+C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

7.7.
Estimate
N_{4}:=(\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST})(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST})

Recall that
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{P}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})=(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})
, and
\mathcal{Q}_{ST}=\mathcal{P}_{ST}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})=((D_{t}^{ST})^{2}-iA_{ST}\partial_{\alpha})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})
. Taking the difference, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}_{ST}=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{P}-\mathcal{P}_{ST})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})-\mathcal{P}_{ST}(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}).

By a direct computation we can write

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}-\mathcal{P}_{ST}=

\displaystyle((\partial_{t}+b\partial_{\alpha})^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})-((\partial_{t}+b_{ST}\partial_{\alpha})^{2}-iA_{ST}\partial_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{t}(D_{t}-D_{t}^{ST})+(D_{t}-D_{t}^{ST})D_{t}-i(A-A_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{t}(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}+(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{ST}-i(A-A_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}.

From computations from previous sections, we have the following:

(7.95)

\left\lVert D_{t}(b-b_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},

(7.96)

\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},

(7.97)

\left\lVert D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST})\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{4},

(7.98)

\left\lVert D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{4}q^{1/2},

(7.99)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{ST}(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST})\right\rVert_{W^{s,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{4}.

Therefore, one has

(7.100)

\left\lVert-i(A-A_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

(7.101)

\left\lVert(\mathcal{P}-\mathcal{P}_{ST})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},

and

(7.102)

\left\lVert\mathcal{P}_{ST}(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\theta_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Hence, we conclude

(7.103)

\left\lVert N_{4}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

7.8.
Estimate
N_{3}:=(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})(\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\tilde{\theta})

We can write

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{P}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}).

Taking the difference, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}=

\displaystyle\Big(D_{t}(b-\tilde{b})\Big)\partial_{\alpha}+(b-\tilde{b})D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}+(b-\tilde{b})\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{D}_{t}-i(A-\tilde{A})\partial_{\alpha}.

Using the similar argument to that for
N_{4}
, we obtain

(7.104)

\left\lVert N_{3}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

7.9.
Estimate
N_{2}:=\Big(\mathcal{Q}_{ST}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})\Big)\tilde{\theta}_{ST}

Taking the differences, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{ST}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{ST}-(\mathcal{Q}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{(\mathcal{P}_{ST}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST}(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}})\Big\}-\Big\{(\mathcal{P}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{(\mathcal{P}_{ST}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})-(\mathcal{P}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\Big\}+\Big\{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST}(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}})\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big\{(\mathcal{P}-\mathcal{P}_{ST})-(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST})\Big\}(I-\mathcal{H_{\zeta}})+\Big\{(\mathcal{P}_{ST}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST})(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})\Big\}

\displaystyle+(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST})(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})+\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST}\Big(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}-(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}})\Big)

and

\displaystyle\Big((\mathcal{P}-\mathcal{P}_{ST})-(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST})\Big)(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big(D_{t}(b-b_{ST})\Big)\partial_{\alpha}+(b-b_{ST})D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}+(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{ST}-i(A-A_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}\Big\}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}

\displaystyle-\Big\{\Big(\tilde{D}_{t}(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\Big)\partial_{\alpha}+(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\tilde{D}_{t}\partial_{\alpha}+(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}-i(\tilde{A}-\tilde{A}_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}\Big\}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big(D_{t}(b-b_{ST})\Big)\partial_{\alpha}-\Big(\tilde{D}_{t}(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\Big)\partial_{\alpha}\Big\}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}

\displaystyle+\Big\{(b-b_{ST})D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}-(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\tilde{D}_{t}\partial_{\alpha}\Big\}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}

\displaystyle+\Big\{(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{ST}-(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\Big\}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}

\displaystyle-i\Big(A-A_{ST}-(\tilde{A}-\tilde{A}_{ST})\Big)\partial_{\alpha}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

We estimate each term on the right-hand side of the expression above.

By (
7.70
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{1}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq\left\lVert(D_{t}(b-b_{ST})-\tilde{D}_{t}(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Note that
I_{2}
can be written as

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\Big\{(b-b_{ST})D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}-(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\tilde{D}_{t}\partial_{\alpha}\Big\}\tilde{\theta}_{ST}-\Big[(b-b_{ST})D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}-(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\tilde{D}_{t}\partial_{\alpha},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}\Big]\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}.

Using (
7.43
) and (
7.33
), one has

\displaystyle\left\lVert I_{2}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Big((b-b_{ST})-(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})\Big)D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert(\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST})(b-\tilde{b})\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

We estimate for
I_{3}
by the same way as for
I_{2}
, and obtain

(7.105)

\left\lVert I_{3}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Using (
7.76
) and
\tilde{A}=\tilde{A}_{ST}=1
, we have

(7.106)

\left\lVert I_{4}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Therefore, we can conclude

(7.107)

\left\lVert\Big((\mathcal{P}-\mathcal{P}_{ST})-(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST})\Big)(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Similarly, we also have

(7.108)

\left\lVert(\mathcal{P}_{ST}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST})(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},

(7.109)

\left\lVert(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST})(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

The quantity
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST}\Big(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}-(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}})\Big)\tilde{\theta}_{ST}
can be written as

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST}\Big(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}-(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}})\Big)\tilde{\theta}_{ST}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}-(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}})\Big)\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST}\tilde{\theta}_{ST}+[\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}-(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}})]\tilde{\theta}_{ST}

Using the above identity and Proposition
2.3
, we obtain

(7.110)

\begin{split}&\left\lVert\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{ST}\Big(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}-(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}})\Big)\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
