\leq&C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\theta-\tilde{\theta}-(\theta_{ST}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST}))\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\theta}_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s,\infty}}+C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.\end{split}

Hence, finally, we achieve

(7.111)

\left\lVert N_{2}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

7.10.
Estimate
M_{1}:=D_{t}N_{1}

Recall that

(7.112)

M_{1}=D_{t}(G-G_{ST}-(\tilde{G}-\tilde{G}_{ST})).

Recall that
G=G_{1}+G_{2}
. We first estimate
\left\lVert D_{t}(G_{1}-G_{1,ST}-(\tilde{G}_{1}-\tilde{G}_{1,ST}))\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}
. The estimate for
\left\lVert D_{t}(G_{2}-G_{2,ST}-(\tilde{G}_{2}-\tilde{G}_{2,ST}))\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}
follows from the similar yet even easier calculations.

Using (
7.89
), we have

\displaystyle D_{t}G_{1}=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{q\pi}D_{t}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{\Im\Big\{\cos(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})\overline{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}\Big\}}{\Big|\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})\Big|^{2}}(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t))\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)\,d\beta.

Let
\kappa:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be the diffeomorphism be defined by

Changing of variables, we get

\displaystyle D_{t}G_{1}(\alpha,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{\Im\Big\{\cos(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})\overline{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}\Big\}}{\Big|\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})\Big|^{2}}(D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t))\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)\,d\beta

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{\Im\Big\{\cos(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})\overline{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}\Big\}}{\Big|\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})\Big|^{2}}(D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t))\partial_{\beta}D_{t}^{2}\zeta(\beta,t)\,d\beta

\displaystyle:=

Since

\begin{split}&\Im\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)}{\sin^{2}(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}\Big)\\

so

is essentially of the same type of

in (

). Using the same argument for

, we obtain

\left\lVert D_{t}G-D_{t}^{ST}G_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{G}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{G}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

We can write

M_{1}=\Big(D_{t}G-D_{t}^{ST}G_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{G}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{G}_{ST})\Big)+(D_{t}-D_{t}^{ST})G_{ST}+(D_{t}-\tilde{D}_{t})\tilde{G}-(D_{t}-\tilde{D}_{ST})\tilde{G}_{ST},

and

\displaystyle(D_{t}-D_{t}^{ST})G_{ST}+(D_{t}-\tilde{D}_{t})\tilde{G}-(D_{t}-\tilde{D}_{ST})\tilde{G}_{ST}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}G_{ST}+\Big((D_{t}-\tilde{D}_{t})-(D_{t}-\tilde{D}_{ST})\Big)\tilde{G}+(D_{t}-\tilde{D}_{ST})(\tilde{G}-\tilde{G}_{ST})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}G_{ST}+(\tilde{b}_{ST}-\tilde{b})\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{G}+(b-\tilde{b}_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{G}-\tilde{G}_{ST}).

Then we can bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert M_{1}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Big(D_{t}G-D_{t}^{ST}G_{ST}-(\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{G}-\tilde{D}_{t}^{ST}\tilde{G}_{ST})\Big)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert(b-b_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}G_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert(\tilde{b}_{ST}-\tilde{b})\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{G}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert(b-\tilde{b}_{ST})\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{G}-\tilde{G}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

Using

(7.116)

\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert\tilde{b}-\tilde{b}_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},

(7.117)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}G_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s,\infty}}+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{G}\right\rVert_{W^{s,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{3},

and

(7.118)

\left\lVert(b-\tilde{b}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}q^{1/2},\quad\quad\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{G}-\tilde{G}_{ST})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}q^{-1/2},

we conclude that

(7.119)

\left\lVert M_{1}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

7.11.
Estimate

Recall that

We can bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert M_{5}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{H^{s}}\left\lVert A\zeta_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\left\lVert\rho_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}

2.1
, and Lemma
7.4
, one has

\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

Recall that

. We decompose

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}[D_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\rho=

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\it{II}_{1}+\it{II}_{2}+\it{II}_{3}+\it{II}_{4}+\it{II}_{5}.

By Proposition
2.1
and the estimates for

:

we obtain

For
\it{II}_{3}
and

\displaystyle\left\lVert\it{II}_{3}+\it{II}_{4}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert A\zeta_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert\rho_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}+\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert\rho_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert A-1\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert\rho_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

For
\it{II}_{5}
, by (
2.23
), we obtain

\displaystyle[\mathcal{P},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]D_{t}\rho=

\displaystyle[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]D_{t}\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{4\pi q^{2}i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)}{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}\Big)^{2}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\rho(\beta,t)\,d\beta

For

, using Proposition
2.1
, one has

(7.131)

\left\lVert\it{II}_{52}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

For

, we decompose

as

D_{t}\rho=\frac{1}{2}(I+\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}})D_{t}\rho+\frac{1}{2}(I-\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}})D_{t}\rho:=\mathbb{P}_{A}D_{t}\rho+\mathbb{P}_{H}D_{t}\rho.

Then we can write

(7.133)

\it{II}_{51}=2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}_{A}D_{t}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}_{H}D_{t}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}:=\it{II}_{511}+\it{II}_{512}.

For
\it{II}_{511}
, using
5
5

5

Note that
\mathbb{P}_{A}D_{t}\rho=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})D_{t}\bar{\rho}
is the boundary value of a bounded holomorphic function in
\Omega(t)
, so
\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\overline{\mathbb{P}_{A}D_{t}\rho}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}
is also the boundary value of a bounded holomorphic function in

. So

is the boundary value of a bounded holomorphic function in
\Omega(t)
which approaches zero as

.

(7.134)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})D_{t}\bar{\zeta}=0,\,\,(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})D_{t}\bar{\zeta}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\overline{\mathbb{P}_{A}D_{t}\rho}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}=0.

we have

\displaystyle\it{II}_{511}=

\displaystyle 2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\mathbb{P}_{A}D_{t}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

Then we can use arguments as for

in §

to conclude that

For
\it{II}_{512}
, we decompose it as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{H}D_{t}\rho=D_{t}\mathbb{P}_{H}\rho+[\mathbb{P}_{H},D_{t}]\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}D_{t}(I-\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST}))-\frac{1}{2}[D_{t}\bar{\zeta},\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\rho}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}D_{t}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST}))-\frac{1}{2}D_{t}(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}+\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\rho-\frac{1}{2}[D_{t}\bar{\zeta},\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\rho}{\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle F_{1}+F_{2}+F_{3}.

For the first term, note that by Corollary
A.1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c(t),

where

(7.136)

c(t)=\frac{1}{q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\alpha}\zeta(\beta,t)(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST}))\,d\alpha.

For
F_{2}
and
F_{3}
, it’s easy to obtain

(7.137)

\left\lVert F_{2}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert F_{3}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

From the computations above, it follows

(7.138)

\begin{split}\left\lVert\it{II}_{512}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}=&\left\lVert 2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(c^{\prime}(t)+F_{2}+F_{3})}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.\end{split}

So we obtain

(7.139)

\left\lVert\it{II}_{5}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

To estimate
\it{II}_{2}
, we use (
2.23
) to rewrite
D_{t}[\mathcal{P},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\rho
as

\displaystyle D_{t}[\mathcal{P},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\rho=

\displaystyle D_{t}\Big\{2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{4\pi q^{2}i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)}{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}\Big)^{2}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\rho(\beta,t)\,d\beta\Big\}.

Then
D_{t}[\mathcal{P},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\rho
can be written as

(7.140)

D_{t}[\mathcal{P},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\rho=2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+2[D_{t}^{2}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+g,

where

\displaystyle g=

\displaystyle D_{t}\Big\{-\frac{1}{4\pi q^{2}i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)}{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}\Big)^{2}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\rho(\beta,t)\,d\beta\Big\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4q^{2}\pi i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha,t)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta,t)}{\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})}\Big)^{2}\partial_{\beta}D_{t}\rho(\beta,t)\,d\beta

The calculation and the estimates for

is similar to the term

, see §

. We obtain

\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

For
2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}^{2}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+2[D_{t}^{2}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}
, decomposing

\rho=\mathbb{P}_{H}\rho+\mathbb{P}_{A}\rho,

and using the same arguments as for

, we obtain

(7.143)

\left\lVert\it{II}_{2}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

Therefore, we finally conclude that

(7.144)

\left\lVert M_{6}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

7.14.
Summary of the estimates

To conclude, by assuming the a priori assumptions (
7.2
) and (
7.3
), together with Lemma
7.5
, we obtain

(7.145)

\begin{split}&\sum_{j=1}^{4}\left\lVert N_{j}(t)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+\sum_{j=1}^{6}\left\lVert M_{j}(t)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon^{2}E_{s}^{1/2}(t)\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.\end{split}

(7.146)

\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},

and

(7.147)

\left\lVert b-b_{ST}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}q^{-1/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},

8.
Energy estimates

The goal of this section is to obtain the following energy estimates.

Proposition 8.1
.

Assuming the a priori assumptions (
7.2
) and (
7.3
), we have for
t\in[0,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]
,

(8.1)

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{E}(t)\leq C\epsilon^{5}\delta^{2}e^{2\epsilon^{2}t},

where
C>0
is some constant depending on
s
only.

Recall that

(8.2)

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{E}_{n}(t)=&\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{2}{A}\Re(D_{t}\rho^{(n)}\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{1,n})-\frac{1}{A}\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}|D_{t}\rho^{(n)}|^{2}\,d\alpha\\
&+2\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\phi}^{(n)}+\partial_{t}\mathcal{\phi}^{(n)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{(n)}+\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{(n)}\,d\alpha\\
:=&\mathfrak{E}_{1,n}+\mathfrak{E}_{2,n}+\mathfrak{E}_{3,n}+\mathfrak{E}_{4,n}+\mathfrak{E}_{5,n},\end{split}

where

(8.3)

\mathcal{C}_{1,n}=\sum_{m=1}^{4}\partial_{\alpha}^{n}N_{m}+[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\rho,

and

(8.4)

\rho^{(n)}=\partial_{\alpha}^{n}\rho,\quad\phi^{(n)}=\frac{1}{2}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\rho^{n},\quad\quad\mathcal{R}^{(n)}=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\rho^{(n)}.

From the computations in previous section, we have obtained the estimates for
N_{m}
. To close the energy estimates for
\mathcal{E}_{n}(t)
, we still need to obtain the estimates for
\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}|D_{t}\rho^{(n)}[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\rho|
, and
\mathfrak{E}_{j},j=2,3,4,5
.

Recall also that

(8.5)

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{F}_{n}(t)=&\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{2}{A}\Re(D_{t}\sigma^{(n)}\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{2,n})-\frac{1}{A}\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}|D_{t}\sigma^{(n)}|^{2}\,d\alpha\\
:=&\mathfrak{F}_{1}+\mathfrak{F}_{2}.\end{split}

where

(8.6)

\mathcal{C}_{2,n}=\partial_{\alpha}^{n}(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\sigma+[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\sigma

8.1.
Estimate
\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}|D_{t}\rho^{(n)}[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\rho|\,d\alpha
and
\mathcal{C}_{1,n}

By direct commutator computations, we have

(8.7)

\begin{split}&[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\rho\\
=&\sum_{m=1}^{n}\partial_{\alpha}^{n-m}[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}]\partial_{\alpha}^{m-1}\rho\\
=&\sum_{m=1}^{n}\partial_{\alpha}^{n-m}(D_{t}[D_{t},\partial_{\alpha}]+[D_{t},\partial_{\alpha}]D_{t}+iA_{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha})\partial_{\alpha}^{m-1}\rho\\
=&-\sum_{m=1}^{n}\partial_{\alpha}^{n-m}D_{t}(b_{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha})\partial_{\alpha}^{m-1}\rho-\sum_{m=1}^{n}\partial_{\alpha}^{n-m}b_{\alpha}D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}^{m-1}\rho+i\sum_{m=1}^{n}\partial_{\alpha}^{n-m}A_{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}^{m}\rho.\end{split}

By writing
D_{t}(b_{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha})
and
D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}^{m-1}\rho
as

(8.8)

D_{t}(b_{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha})=(D_{t}b_{\alpha})\partial_{\alpha}+b_{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}+b_{\alpha}[D_{t},\partial_{\alpha}],

(8.9)

D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}^{m-1}\rho=\partial_{\alpha}^{m-1}D_{t}\rho+[D_{t},\partial_{\alpha}^{m-1}]\rho,

and using the estimates

(8.10)

\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}q^{1/2},\quad\quad\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2},\quad\left\lVert D_{t}b\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2},

and

(8.11)

\left\lVert A-1\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3}q^{1/2},\quad\quad\left\lVert A-1\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3},

we obtain

(8.12)

\left\lVert[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

Therefore, we conclude that

(8.13)

\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}|D_{t}\rho^{(n)}[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\rho\,d\alpha\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

By (
8.13
), (
7.145
) and the Cauchy Schwarz inequality, one has

(8.14)

|\mathcal{C}_{1,n}|\leq C\epsilon^{5}\delta^{2}e^{2\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon^{2}(\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}).

8.2.
Estimate
\mathfrak{E}_{2}

For
\mathfrak{E}_{2}
, recalling that by (
7.121
) and (
7.122
),

(8.15)

\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}q^{1/2},\quad\quad\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}\leq C\epsilon^{2},

together with (
7.73
), one has

(8.16)

\begin{split}|\mathfrak{E}_{2}|\leq&C\left\lVert\frac{1}{A}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\Big\{\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}}^{2}+\left\lVert\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}\right\rVert_{W^{s-1,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}\Big\}\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.\end{split}

8.3.
Estimates
\mathfrak{E}_{3}
,
\mathfrak{E}_{4}
, and
\mathfrak{E}_{5}

8.3.1.
Estimate
\phi^{(n)}
and
\mathcal{R}^{(n)}

Recall that
\mathfrak{E}_{3}=2\Im\int\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\phi}^{(n)}\,d\alpha
.
We have for
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{n}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\rho-\frac{1}{2}[\partial_{\alpha}^{n},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\rho

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{n}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST}))-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\partial_{\alpha}^{n-m}[\zeta_{\alpha}-1,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{m}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}^{n}c(t)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\partial_{\alpha}^{n-m}[\zeta_{\alpha}-1,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{m}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\partial_{\alpha}^{n-m}[\zeta_{\alpha}-1,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{m}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}.

Here, we used the fact that
\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST}))=c(t)
for some constant
c(t)
, and therefore
6
6

6

Here and after, we use
c(t)
to denote constants depending on
t
only.

(8.17)

\partial_{\alpha}^{n}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST}))=0.

So we obtain for
1\leq n\leq s+1
,

(8.18)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})},

and for
\mathcal{R}_{t}^{(n)}
, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{t}^{(n)}=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\partial_{\alpha}^{n-m}[\zeta_{\alpha}-1,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{m}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}

Then one obtain

(8.19)

\left\lVert\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}.

For
n=0
, we have

(8.20)

\mathcal{R}^{(0)}=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\rho=c(t),\quad\quad\phi^{(0)}=\frac{1}{2}(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\rho.

8.3.2.
Estimate
\mathfrak{E}_{3}

Recll that
\mathfrak{E}_{3}=2\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\phi}^{(n)}\,d\alpha
. For
n=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{3}=2\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(0)}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\phi}^{(0)}\,d\alpha=2\Im c(t)\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\alpha}\bar{\phi}^{(0)}\,d\alpha=0.

For
1\leq n\leq s+1
, using
\mathcal{R}^{(n)}=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\mathcal{R}^{(n)}+c(t)
and the fact
\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\overline{\partial_{\alpha}\phi^{(n)}}d\alpha=0
, one has

(8.21)

\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\overline{\partial_{\alpha}\phi^{(n)}}\,d\alpha=\frac{1}{2}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{t}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\overline{\partial_{\alpha}\phi^{(n)}}\,d\alpha.

We rewrite
\partial_{t}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\mathcal{R}^{(n)}
and
\partial_{\alpha}\phi^{(n)}
as

(8.22)

\partial_{t}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\mathcal{R}^{(n)}=(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}+[\zeta_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}}{\zeta_{\alpha}},

and

(8.23)

\partial_{\alpha}\phi^{(n)}=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\rho-[\zeta_{\alpha},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}.

Therefore, we rewrite

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{3}=

\displaystyle\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{t}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\overline{(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\rho}\,d\alpha-\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{t}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\overline{[\zeta_{\alpha},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\rho}{\zeta_{\alpha}}}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{31}+\mathfrak{E}_{32}.

For
\mathfrak{E}_{32}
, using (
8.19
) and Proposition
2.1
, we have

(8.24)

|\mathfrak{E}_{32}|\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

For
\mathfrak{E}_{31}
, one has

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{31}=

\displaystyle\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\overline{(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\rho}\,d\alpha-\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}[\partial_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}\overline{(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\rho}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{311}+\mathfrak{E}_{312}.

Using the same argument as for
\mathfrak{E}_{32}
, we have

(8.25)

|\mathfrak{E}_{312}|\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

For
\mathfrak{E}_{311}
, we split it into two pieces

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{311}=

\displaystyle\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big((I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\Big)(I-\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big((I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\Big)(I+H_{0})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\Im\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big((I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\Big)(H_{0}+\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{311}^{(1)}+\mathcal{C}_{311}^{(2)}.

Note that since

(8.26)

\left\lVert(H_{0}+\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})},

one has

(8.27)

|\mathfrak{E}_{311}^{(2)}|\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

For
\mathfrak{E}_{311}^{(1)}
, we further decompose it as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{311}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\Im\int\Big((I+H_{0})\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\Big)\Big((I+H_{0})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\Big)\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\Im\int\Big((\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-H_{0})\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\Big)\Big((I+H_{0})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\Big)\,d\alpha

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle L_{1}+L_{2}.

Clearly, using (
8.19
), we have

\left\lVert(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}-H_{0})\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon^{4}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})},

from which it follows

(8.28)

|L_{2}|\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

Finally, we analyze
L_{1}
. Note that
(I+H_{0})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\bar{\rho}=\partial_{\alpha}(I+H_{0})\partial_{\alpha}^{n}\bar{\rho}
, and
(I+H_{0})\partial_{\alpha}^{n}\bar{\rho}
is the boundary value of a holomorphic function
\Phi_{2}(x+iy,t)
in
\mathbb{P}_{-}
, so
(I+H_{0})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\bar{\rho}
is the boundary value of
\partial_{z}\Phi_{2}
. Notice that
\partial_{z}\Phi_{2}(x+iy,t)\rightarrow 0
as
y\rightarrow-\infty
. Therefore,

\Big((I+H_{0})\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}\Big)\Big((I+H_{0})\partial_{\alpha}^{n+1}\bar{\rho}\Big)

is the boundary value of a holomorphic function
\Phi_{3}(x+iy,t)
, with

(8.29)

\Phi_{3}(x+iy,t)\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{as}\penalty\ y\rightarrow-\infty.

Applying Cauchy’s theorem, one has

(8.30)

L_{1}=0.

Therefore we conclude that

(8.31)

|\mathfrak{E}_{3}|\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

8.3.3.
Estimate
\mathfrak{E}_{4}

Integration by parts, the estimate for
\mathfrak{E}_{4}
is similar to that for
\mathfrak{E}_{3}
. Hence we obtain

(8.32)

|\mathfrak{E}_{4}|\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

8.3.4.
Estimate
\mathfrak{E}_{5}

Using (
8.18
) and (
8.19
), we obtain

(8.33)

|\mathfrak{E}_{5}|\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

8.4.
Estimate for
\sum_{n=0}^{s}\frac{d\mathcal{E}_{n}}{dt}

Putting all computations from previous subsections together, we conclude that

(8.34)

\sum_{n=0}^{s}\frac{d\mathcal{E}_{n}}{dt}\leq C\epsilon^{5}\delta^{2}e^{2\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

8.5.
Estimate
\mathcal{C}_{2,n}

Recall that
\mathcal{C}_{2,n}=\partial_{\alpha}^{n}(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\sigma+[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\sigma
. First we estimate
\partial_{\alpha}^{n}(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\sigma
. Recall that
(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\sigma=\sum_{j=1}^{n}M_{j}
. By (
7.145
), we have

(8.35)

\sum_{n=0}^{s}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}^{n}(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\sigma\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon^{2}E_{s}^{1/2}(t).

We estimate
[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\sigma
by a way similar to
[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\rho
in §
8.1
. We obtain

(8.36)

\left\lVert[D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{n}]\sigma\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

Therefore, we conclude that

(8.37)

\begin{split}|\mathfrak{F}_{1}|=&\Big|\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{2}{A}\Re(D_{t}\sigma^{(n)}\bar{\mathcal{C}}_{2,n})\Big|\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{5}\delta^{2}e^{2\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.\end{split}

8.6.
Estimate
\mathfrak{F}_{2}

By direct computations, we have

(8.38)

\begin{split}|\mathfrak{F}_{2}|=&\Big|\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\frac{1}{A}\frac{a_{t}}{a}\circ\kappa^{-1}|D_{t}\sigma^{(n)}|^{2}\,d\alpha\Big|\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}.\end{split}

8.7.
Conclude the proof of Proposition
8.1

From computations in previous subsections, we have that for
t\in[0,T_{0}]
,

(8.39)

\begin{split}&\sum_{n=0}^{s}\frac{d}{dt}(\mathcal{E}_{n}(t)+\mathcal{F}_{n}(t))\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{5}\delta^{2}e^{2\epsilon^{2}t}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}^{2}+C\epsilon^{2}E_{s}(t).\end{split}

By Lemma
7.5
and the a priori assumption (
7.2
) and (
7.3
), one has

(8.40)

\left\lVert D_{t}\rho-2D_{t}r\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon E(t)^{1/2}+C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},

(8.41)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}r\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C(\epsilon E_{s}^{1/2}+\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}\epsilon^{5/2})\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

By the definition of
\rho
, one has

(8.42)

\begin{split}\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}\rho\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq&C\epsilon\left\lVert D_{t}r\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+C\epsilon\left\lVert r_{\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}+2\left\lVert D_{t}^{2}r\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.\end{split}

Finally, we can conclude that

(8.43)

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{E}(t)\leq C\epsilon^{5}\delta^{2}e^{2\epsilon^{2}t},

which proves Proposition
8.1
.

8.8.
Equivalence of
E_{s}(t)
and
\mathcal{E}(t)

We can show that under the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
) and the assumption (
7.3
), for
0\leq t\leq T_{0}
,

(8.44)

E_{s}^{1/2}(t)\leq C\mathcal{E}(t)^{1/2}+C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

The proof here is similar to §9.16 in
[
62
]
, so we omit the details.

8.9.
Control the growth of the error term

By Proposition
8.1
and (
8.44
), we obtain the following energy estimate.

Proposition 8.2
.

With notations above, under the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
) and the assumption (
7.3
), for
0\leq t\leq T_{0}
, we have

(8.45)

E_{s}(t)\leq E_{s}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}C\epsilon^{5}\delta^{2}e^{2\epsilon^{2}\tau}\,d\tau.

In particular, a direct computation of the time integral gives

(8.46)

E_{s}(t)\leq E_{s}(0)+C\epsilon^{3}\delta^{2}(e^{\epsilon^{2}t}-1).

for
0\leq t\leq T_{0}
.

9.
Modulational instability of the Stokes waves

In this final section, we prove the nonlinear modulational instability of the Stokes wave. To achieve this goal, the essential step is to use the energy estimates obtained from the previous section to establish the long-time existence of the reminder term.

9.1.
Existence of initial data with desired properties

Notice that the approximate solution
\zeta_{app}
, (
6.2
), we obtained in Section
5
could not be taken as the initial data for the system (
3.1
) since it does not satisfy the holomorphic conditions in the equation.

We should first construct initial data to the water wave system (
3.1
) for long-wave perturbations of the Stokes wave
\zeta_{ST}
.

Proposition 9.1
.

Suppose that
B_{0}\in H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})
, and
\|B_{0}-i\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}\leq C_{0}
for some absolute constant
C_{0}
. In addition, we assume that

(9.1)

\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}B_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)e^{i\alpha}\,d\alpha=0

Then
there exist
\zeta_{0}
and
v_{0}
such that
\zeta_{0}-\alpha\in H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})
and
v_{0}\in H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})
satisfying

(9.2)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}})(\bar{\zeta}_{0}-\alpha)=0,

(9.3)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}})\bar{v}_{0}=0,

and

(9.4)

\left\lVert\zeta_{0}-\zeta_{ST}(\alpha,0)-\epsilon(B(\epsilon\alpha)-i)e^{i\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\|B_{0}(\alpha)-i\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

(9.5)

\|v_{0}-D_{t}^{ST}\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)-i\omega\epsilon(B(\epsilon\alpha)-i)e^{i\alpha}\|_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\|B_{0}(\alpha)-i\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})},

for some constant
C
depending on
C_{0}
and
s
.

Proof.

The initial data here is obtained by iteration. We start by defining

\zeta_{1}:=\zeta_{ST}(\alpha,0)+\epsilon(B_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)-i)e^{i\alpha}.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{1}}
be the Hilbert transform associated with
\zeta_{1}
. Define
\zeta_{2}
by

(9.6)

\bar{\zeta}_{2}(\alpha)-\alpha:=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{1}})\Big(\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\cdot,0)-\alpha+\epsilon(\bar{B}_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)+i)e^{-i\alpha}\Big).

Assuming that
\zeta_{n}
has been constructed, we define

(9.7)

\bar{\zeta}_{n+1}(\alpha)-\alpha:=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{n}})\Big(\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\cdot,0)-\alpha+\epsilon(\bar{B}_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)+i)e^{-i\alpha}\Big).

Since

(9.8)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)})(\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\cdot,0)-\alpha)=0,

and by Lemma
5.1
,

(9.9)

\left\lVert(I-H_{0})(\bar{B}_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)+i)e^{-i\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})},

one has

(9.10)

\small\begin{split}&\left\lVert\bar{\zeta}_{2}-\bar{\zeta}_{1}\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{1}}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)}(\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\cdot,0)-\alpha)\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\left\lVert(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{1}}-H_{0})(\bar{B}_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)+i)e^{-i\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
&+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\left\lVert(I-H_{0})(\bar{B}_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)+i)e^{-i\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0))\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)-\alpha\right\rVert_{W^{s,\infty}(q\mathbb{T})}+C(\epsilon^{3/2}+\epsilon^{7/2})\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&C\epsilon^{3/2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.\end{split}

It is straightforward to obtain

(9.11)

\|\zeta_{n+1}-\zeta_{n}\|_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon\|\zeta_{n}-\zeta_{n-1}\|_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}.

Therefore, after applying the fixed point theorem,
\zeta_{n+1}-\alpha
converges in
H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})
to a function
\xi\in H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})
. Define

\gamma(\alpha)
as

(9.12)

\gamma:=\alpha+\xi(\alpha,0).

By the definition of
\gamma
and the iteration procedure above, there hold

(9.13)

\left\lVert\gamma-\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

(9.14)

\left\lVert\gamma-\Big(\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)+\epsilon(B_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)-i)e^{i\alpha}\Big)\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

One has

(9.15)

\bar{\gamma}-\alpha=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})\Big(\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\cdot,0)-\alpha+\epsilon(\bar{B}_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)+i)e^{-i\alpha}\Big).

By Lemma
A.1
, we know that

(9.16)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})\Big(\bar{\gamma}(\alpha)-\alpha\Big)=c_{\gamma},

where

c_{\gamma}:=\frac{1}{2q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\alpha}\gamma(\alpha)\Big(\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,0)-\alpha+\epsilon(\bar{B}_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)+i)e^{-i\alpha}\Big)\,d\alpha.

Note that
\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{ST}(\alpha,0)(\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,0)-\alpha)\,d\alpha=0
because
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)})(\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,0)-\alpha)=0
. So one has

\displaystyle|c_{\gamma}|\leq\Big|\frac{1}{2q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\alpha}(\gamma-\zeta_{ST})(\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,0)-\alpha)d\alpha\Big|+\Big|\frac{1}{2q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\epsilon(\bar{B}_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)+i)e^{-i\alpha}d\alpha\Big|:=c_{\gamma,1}+c_{\gamma,2}.

By (
9.14
), we have

(9.17)

c_{\gamma,1}\leq Cq^{-1}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\gamma-\zeta_{ST}(\alpha,0)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(q\mathbb{T})}\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,0)-\alpha)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

By the orthogonal condition (
9.1
), one has

(9.18)

c_{\gamma 2}=0.

Define
\zeta_{0}(\alpha):=\gamma(\alpha)-c_{\gamma}
. Noting that
\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}}=\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}
, we have

\displaystyle(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}})(\bar{\zeta}_{0}-\alpha)=0.

Moreover,

(9.19)

\left\lVert\zeta_{0}-\zeta_{ST}(\alpha,0)\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})},

and

(9.20)

\left\lVert\zeta_{0}-\zeta_{ST}(\alpha,0)-\epsilon(B(\epsilon\alpha)-i)e^{i\alpha}\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\|B_{0}(\alpha)-i\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

Define
v_{0}
as

(9.21)

\bar{v}_{0}(\alpha)=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}})\Big(D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,0)-i\omega\epsilon(\bar{B}_{0}+i)e^{-i\alpha}\Big)+d_{v_{0}},

where

(9.22)

d_{v_{0}}:=-\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\beta}\zeta_{0}\Big(D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\beta,0)-i\omega\epsilon(\bar{B}_{0}+i)e^{-i\beta}\Big)\,d\beta

By Corollary
A.1
, we have

(9.23)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}})\bar{v}_{0}=0.

Note that we can rewrite

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}})D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,0)-\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\zeta_{\beta}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\beta,0)\,d\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)})D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,0)-\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\beta}\zeta_{ST}(\beta,0)D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\beta,0)\,d\beta

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)})D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\cdot,0)-\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\beta}(\zeta_{0}(\beta)-\zeta_{ST}(\beta,0))D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\beta,0)\,d\beta

Since
D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}
is holomorphic and vanishes as
y\rightarrow-\infty
, we have

(9.24)

\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\beta}\zeta_{ST}D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\beta,0)\,d\beta=0,

and

(9.25)

D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\cdot,0)=\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)})D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}.

Therefore, by (
9.19
),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}})D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,0)-\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\zeta_{\beta}D_{t}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\beta,0)\,d\beta-D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\alpha,0)\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}}-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}(\cdot,0)})D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\cdot,0)\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\beta}(\zeta_{0}-\zeta_{ST}(\beta,0))D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\beta,0)\,d\beta\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{3/2}\left\lVert B_{0}-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

By Lemma
5.1
, we can bound

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Big(-\frac{1}{2}i\omega\epsilon(I+H_{0})(\bar{B}_{0}+i)e^{-i\alpha}\Big)-\Big(-i\omega\epsilon(\bar{B}_{0}+i)e^{-i\alpha}\Big)\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{7/2}\left\lVert B_{0}-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Big(-\frac{1}{2}i\omega\epsilon(I+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}})(\bar{B}_{0}+i)e^{-i\alpha}\Big)-\Big(-i\omega\epsilon(\bar{B}_{0}+i)e^{-i\alpha}\Big)\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q^{\prime}\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{7/2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}+\frac{1}{2}\omega\epsilon\left\lVert(\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{0}}-H_{0})(B_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)-i)\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{3/2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

Also, using
q=\epsilon^{-1}q_{1}
, we split

\displaystyle\frac{i\omega\epsilon}{4q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{0}(\bar{B}_{0}+i)e^{-i\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\omega\epsilon}{4q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big|(\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{0}-1)(B_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)-i)\Big|\,d\alpha+\Big|\frac{\omega\epsilon}{4q\pi}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(\bar{B}_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)+i)e^{-i\alpha}\,d\alpha\Big|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I+II.

For
I
, we have

(9.26)

I\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

For
II
, by the orthogonality condition (
9.1
),
one has

(9.27)

II=0.

By (
9.4
), we finally conclude that

\displaystyle\|\bar{v}_{0}-D_{t}^{ST}\bar{\zeta}_{ST}(\cdot,0)+i\omega\epsilon(\bar{B}_{0}(\epsilon\alpha)+i)e^{-i\alpha}\|_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle C\epsilon^{3/2}\|B_{0}(\alpha)-i\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

We are done.
∎

Remark 9.1
.

Indeed, by the asymptotic analysis in §
5
, the initial data
(\zeta_{0},v_{0})
constructed in Proposition
9.1
satisfies

(9.28)

\left\lVert(\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{0}-1,v_{0})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})\times H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

In particular, for
\epsilon
sufficiently small, one has

(9.29)

\left\lVert(\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{0}-1,v_{0})\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})\times H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon^{3/2}\left\lVert B_{0}(\alpha)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}.

9.2.
Extended lifespan

Now we are ready to conclude the result on the long-time existence.

Given a solution
B(X,T)\in H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})
to the NLS

(9.30)

iB_{T}+\frac{1}{8}B_{XX}=\frac{1}{2}|B|^{2}B-\frac{1}{2}B,

we define

(9.31)

\zeta^{(1)}(\alpha,t)=B(\epsilon(\alpha+\frac{1}{2\omega}t),\epsilon^{2}t)e^{i(\alpha+\omega t)}.

9.2.1.
Long-time estimates

By Theorem
3.1
, Proposition
8.2
, Proposition
9.1
, and the standard bootstrap argument, we obtain the following.

Theorem 9.2
.

Let
s\geq 4
be given and
s^{\prime}=s+7
. Let
\zeta_{ST}
be a Stokes wave of period
2\pi
and amplitude
\epsilon
. Let
0<\delta\ll 1
be an arbitrarily small but fixed number. For any given
q\in\mathbb{Q}_{+}
with
q\geq\frac{1}{\epsilon}
, and any solution
B
to the NLS (
9.30
) satisfying

(9.32)

\left\lVert B(\alpha,0)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}\leq\delta,\quad\quad\,q_{1}=\epsilon q

and the orthogonality condition (
9.1
),
there exist
\zeta_{0}
and
v_{0}
such that
(\zeta_{0}-\alpha,v_{0})\in H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})\times H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T})
and they satisfy the estimate

(9.33)

\left\lVert(\zeta_{0},v_{0})-\epsilon(\zeta^{(1)}(\cdot,0),\partial_{t}\zeta^{(1)}(\cdot,0))\right\rVert_{H^{s+1}(q\mathbb{T})\times H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon^{3/2}\delta

where
\zeta^{(1)}
is constructed as (
9.31
).
For all such data
(\zeta_{0},v_{0})
, the water wave system (
1.6
) admits a unique solution
\zeta(\alpha,t)
on
[0,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]
with

(\zeta_{\alpha}-1,D_{t}\zeta)\in C([0,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}];H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})\times H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T}))

satisfying the following estimate

(9.34)

\left\lVert\Big(\partial_{\alpha}\zeta(\alpha,t)-1,D_{t}\zeta\Big)-\epsilon\Big(\partial_{\alpha}\zeta^{(1)},\partial_{t}\zeta^{(1)}\Big)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})\times H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{3/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t},

for all
t\in[0,\,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]
where
\delta\ll\mu<1
is a fixed number.

Proof.

For given
\zeta_{ST}
,
\delta
and
B(\alpha,0)
satisfying (
9.32
),
the existence of
(\zeta_{0},v_{0})
satisfying (
9.33
) is guaranteed by Proposition
9.1
. By Proposition
5.1
, there exists a fixed number
\mu\in(0,1)
uniform in
\delta
and
\epsilon
such that for all solution
B
to the NLS (
9.30
) satisfying (
9.32
), one has

(9.35)

\left\lVert B(\alpha,t)-i\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q_{1}\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\delta e^{t},\quad t\in[0,\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}].

So the existence of
B
satisfying (
7.3
) is guaranteed.

Assume the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
) with constant
C>2
. Clearly, the bootstrap assumption (
7.2
) is satisfied at
t=0
. Using the a priori energy estimates provided in Proposition
8.2
, the estimate (
8.46
), the constant appearing the bootstrap assumption is improved since
E_{s}(0)+C\epsilon^{3}\delta^{2}(e^{\epsilon^{2}t}-1)<C\epsilon^{3}\delta^{2}e^{\epsilon^{2}t}
. Therefore together with the blowup criterion (
3.16
) and (
3.17
), we are able to use the bootstrap argument to prove that the solution
(\zeta_{\alpha}-1,D_{t}\zeta)\in C([0,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}];H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})\times H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T}))
, and (
9.34
) holds.
∎

Remark 9.3
.

Note that by construction, the initial data
(\zeta_{0},v_{0})
is a long-wave perturbation (with fundamental period
2q\pi
) of the Stokes wave
\zeta_{ST}
.

Remark 9.4
.

This theorem also shows the validity of the modulational approximation via NLS. It might be interesting to point out that due to the fact that the Stokes wave is a global solution to the water wave system, the valid time scale
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{1}{\delta})
for the modulational approximation of the perturbed flow is longer than other settings. See for example
[
66
,
62
]
where the valid time scale is of order
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-2})
.

9.3.
Nonlinear instability

With the long-time existence and estimates, we now analyze the instability of Stokes waves under long-wave perturbations.

9.3.1.
Growth of large scales

By Theorem
9.2
, with estimates between the difference of
\zeta-\alpha
and
\epsilon\zeta^{(1)}
, it suffices to analyze the growth of
B
. In this setting, from the proof of the NLS instability, Appendix §
D
, for
B
, we can take the initial data

(9.36)

B(\epsilon\alpha,0)=i+\frac{i}{\sqrt{q_{1}}}\left(\delta_{1}e^{i\frac{k_{0}}{q_{1}}\epsilon\alpha}+\delta_{2}e^{-i\frac{k_{0}}{q_{1}}\epsilon\alpha}+\eta_{1}e^{i\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{q_{1}}}+\eta_{2}e^{-i\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{q_{1}}}\right)

where
k_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
and
\tau
are defined as

(9.37)

\left|\frac{k_{0}}{q_{1}}\right|\sqrt{2-\left|\frac{k_{0}}{q_{1}}\right|^{2}}=\tau:=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\Re\left|\frac{k}{q_{1}}\right|\sqrt{2-\left|\frac{k}{q_{1}}\right|^{2}}

and

\left|\delta_{j}\right|=\frac{\delta}{2s^{\prime}}\ll 1,\,\left|\eta_{j}\right|\ll\left|\delta_{i}\right|

with
\delta
is given as Theorem
9.2
. Note that by construction, (
9.37
),
\tau\leq 1
and
k_{0}\sim q_{1}
.
Clearly,
B(\cdot,0)
satisfies the orthogonality condition (
9.1
).

Furthermore, with initial data above, the solution
B
can be written as

(9.38)

B(\epsilon(\alpha+\frac{1}{2\omega}t),\epsilon^{2}t)=i+\frac{i}{\sqrt{q_{1}}}\left(a_{1}(t)e^{i\epsilon\frac{k_{0}}{q_{1}}(\alpha+\frac{1}{2\omega}t)}+a_{2}(t)e^{-i\epsilon\frac{k_{0}}{q_{1}}(\alpha+\frac{1}{2\omega}t)}+e(X,T)\right),

where
X=\epsilon(\alpha+\frac{1}{2\omega}t),\,T=\epsilon^{2}t
and
a_{j}(t)=a_{j}(0)e^{\tau\epsilon^{2}},\,j=1,2

Then clearly by construction, the
B
above satisfies the perturbation condition (
9.32
).
Denoting

(9.39)

w^{L}(\alpha,t):=\frac{i}{\sqrt{q_{1}}}\Big(a_{1}(t)e^{i\epsilon\frac{k_{0}}{q_{1}}(\alpha+\frac{1}{2\omega}t)}+a_{2}(t)e^{i\epsilon\frac{k_{0}}{q_{1}}(\alpha+\frac{1}{2\omega}t)}\Big)

then
e(X,T)
satisfies additional estimate: for
t\in[0,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]

(9.40)

\left\lVert\frac{1}{\sqrt{q_{1}}}e(X,T)\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert w^{L}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}.

Moreover, at
t_{*}=\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}
, one has

(9.41)

\left\lVert w^{L}(\cdot,t_{*})\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\geq c

for some constant
c\gg\delta
.

Remark 9.5
.

See Subsection §
D.3
from Appendix §
D
for details.

The instability mechanism above is precisely the deriving force of the instability of the Stokes wave.

9.3.2.
The modulational instability

Let
\gamma\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
, and
\phi\geq 0
be given. We use
\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}
to denote the Stokes wave with period
2\pi
, the amplitude
\gamma
, and the phase translation
\phi
. The following result gives the nonlinear instability of Stokes wave.

Corollary 9.1
.

Let
\zeta
be the solution as given in Theorem
9.2
with
B
constructed as (
9.38
). Then we have

(9.42)

\sup_{t\in[0,\,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]}\inf_{\phi\in\mathbb{T}}\inf_{\gamma\in(0,\epsilon_{0})}\left\lVert\Im\{\zeta-\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}\}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(q\mathbb{T})}\geq c\epsilon^{1/2},

for some constant
c>0
which is uniform in
\delta
and
\epsilon
.

In particular, we conclude that the Stokes wave
\zeta_{ST}
given in Theorem
9.2
is modulationally unstable under the long-wave perturbation in
H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})
.

Proof.

First of all, clearly, by construction, the solution
\zeta
given in Theorem
9.2
is a long-wave perturbation of the Stokes wave
\zeta_{ST}
in the same theorem.

We first prove that

(9.43)

\sup_{t\in[0,\,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]}\inf_{\phi\in\mathbb{T}}\inf_{\gamma\in(0,\epsilon_{0})}\left\lVert\Im\{\zeta-\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}\}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}(q\mathbb{T})}\geq c\epsilon^{1/2}.

Recalling that for a Stokes wave of period
2\pi
and amplitude
\gamma
and phase shift
\phi
, we have the asymptotic expansion

(9.44)

\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}-\alpha=i\gamma e^{i(\alpha-\phi)+i\omega_{\gamma}t}+O(\gamma^{2}),\quad\quad\omega_{\gamma}:=1+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}+O(\gamma^{3}).

By estimate (
9.33
) from Theorem
9.2
, to prove (
9.43
), it suffices to show

(9.45)

\sup_{t\in[0,\,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]}\inf_{\phi\in[0,\infty)}\inf_{\gamma\in(0,\epsilon_{0})}\left\lVert\Im\{\epsilon\zeta^{(1)}-\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}\}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}(q\mathbb{T})}\geq 2c\epsilon^{1/2}.

From the explicit formula of
\zeta^{(1)}
, it suffices to prove

(9.46)

\sup_{t\in[0,\,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]}\inf_{\phi\in[0,\infty)}\inf_{\gamma\in(0,\epsilon_{0})}\left\lVert\Im\{\epsilon w^{L}e^{i\alpha+i\omega t}+i\epsilon e^{i\alpha+i\omega t}-\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}\}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}(q\mathbb{T})}\geq 2c\epsilon^{1/2}.

From the leading order term of the expansion (
9.44
), we note that
\omega^{L}e^{ik\alpha+i\omega t}
is orthogonal to the leading order term of
ie^{ik\alpha+i\omega t}-\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}
. This orthogonality and (
9.41
) together imply (
9.46
) from which (
9.43
) follows.

Finally, notice that the leading order term of
\Im\{\zeta-\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}\}
is given by

\Im\{\epsilon w^{L}e^{i\alpha+i\omega t}+i\epsilon e^{i\alpha+i\omega t}-i\gamma e^{i(\alpha-\phi)+i\omega_{\gamma}t}\}

whose Fourier modes are supported around
1
.
Therefore, the estimate (
9.43
) implies (
9.42
).
∎

Remark 9.6
.

From our explicit construction
w^{L}
, the instability here also holds pointwisely.

Remark 9.7
.

Here, we just picked one special
w^{L}
and (
9.38
). We should point out that there are plenty of choices to construct the unstable perturbations since each unstable solution from the scale of NLS can produce a corresponding unstable solution for the water wave system.

9.3.3.
Nonlinear modulational instability in Eulerian coordinates

We can translate the instability estimate (
9.42
) from Corollary
9.1
to Eulerian coordinates.

We take
\zeta
from Corollary
9.1
. Then denote

(9.47)

x(\alpha,t):=\Re\{\zeta(\alpha,t)\}.

Since
\left\lVert\zeta_{\alpha}-1\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}
,
x(\cdot,t):\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defines a diffeomorphism. We can find the inverse of
x(\cdot,t)
as
\alpha(\cdot,t):\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfying

(9.48)

\sup_{t\in[0,\,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]}\left\lVert\alpha_{x}(\cdot,t)-1\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}.

In Eulearian coordinates, the elevation of the perturbed flow is given by

(9.49)

\eta(x,t):=\Im\{\zeta(\alpha(x,t),t)\}.

and the elevation of Stokes waves is defined as

(9.50)

\eta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}(x,t):=\Im\{\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}(\alpha_{ST}(x,t),t)\}.

Corollary 9.2
.

With notations above, we have

(9.51)

\sup_{t\in[0,\,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]}\inf_{\phi\in\mathbb{T}}\inf_{\gamma\in(0,\epsilon_{0})}\left\lVert\eta(\cdot,t)-\eta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(q\mathbb{T})}\geq c\epsilon^{1/2},

for some constant
c>0
which is uniform in
\delta
and
\epsilon
.

Proof.

We again start with the instability in
\dot{H}^{1}(q_{1}\pi)
. We claim that

(9.52)

\sup_{t\in[0,\,\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}]}\inf_{\phi\in\mathbb{T}}\inf_{\gamma\in(0,\epsilon_{0})}\left\lVert\eta(\cdot,t)-\eta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}(\cdot,t)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}(q\mathbb{T})}\geq c\epsilon^{1/2},

Given the
\zeta
from Corollary
9.1
and a fixed Stokes wave
\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}
, we first observe that from estimate (
9.33
) and the construction of
w^{L}
, we know that when
t\sim\epsilon^{-2}\log\frac{\mu}{\delta}
,

\left\lVert\partial_{x}\Im\{\zeta(t)\}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(q\mathbb{T})}\sim\epsilon^{1/2}.

On the other hand
\left\lVert\partial_{x}\Im\{\zeta^{\gamma,\phi}_{ST}(t)\}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(q\mathbb{T})}\sim\gamma
. Therefore, when
\gamma\ll\epsilon
or
\gamma\gg\epsilon
, then clearly, (
9.52
) holds.

It remains to consider
\gamma\sim\epsilon
. In this case we write

(9.53)

\eta(\cdot,t)-\eta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}(\cdot,t)=\Im\{\zeta(\alpha(\cdot,t),t)-\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}(\alpha(\cdot,t),t)\}+\Im\{\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}(\alpha(\cdot,t),t)-\zeta_{ST}^{\gamma,\phi}(\alpha_{ST}(\cdot,t),t)\}

whose leading order terms are given

(9.54)

\Im\{\epsilon w^{L}(\alpha,t)e^{i\alpha+i\omega t}+i\epsilon e^{i\alpha+i\omega t}-i\gamma e^{i(\alpha-\phi)+i\omega_{\gamma}t}\}+\Im\{i\gamma e^{i(\alpha-\phi)+i\omega_{\gamma}t}-i\gamma e^{i(\alpha_{ST}-\phi)+i\omega_{\gamma}t}\}.

Applying
\partial_{x}
to (
9.54
) and then we take the
L^{2}
norm. The lower bound for the first term

(9.55)

\left\lVert\partial_{x}\Im\{\epsilon w^{L}(\alpha,t)e^{i\alpha+i\omega t}+i\epsilon e^{i\alpha+i\omega t}-i\gamma e^{i(\alpha-\phi)+i\omega_{\gamma}t}\}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(q\mathbb{T})}\geq 2c\epsilon^{1/2}

follows from (
9.46
) after applying the change of variable
\alpha
with (
9.48
).

For the second part,when
\gamma\sim\epsilon
, we have the upper bound

(9.56)

\left\lVert\partial_{x}\Im\{i\gamma e^{i(\alpha-\phi)+i\omega_{\gamma}t}-i\gamma e^{i(\alpha_{ST}-\phi)+i\omega_{\gamma}t}\}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}

from (
9.48
) and the similar one for
\alpha_{ST}
. This is of higher order in
\epsilon
.

Therefore, taking the leading order terms and the applying a simple triangle inequality, from (
9.55
) and (
9.56
), we get (
9.52
).

Finally, by the same argument as for Corollary
9.1
, using the support of Fourier modes, the
L^{2}
version (
9.51
) follows from (
9.52
). Indeed, we can write
\alpha
and
\alpha_{ST}
as
x+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
. Then we expand
e^{i\alpha}
and
e^{i\alpha_{ST}}
in terms of powers of
\epsilon
. At the level of
\epsilon
, the Fourier modes of (
9.54
) are supported around
1
. The remaining pieces are of higher orders of
\epsilon
. We omit the details since it is routine. For the expansion of the Stokes wave, we also refer to the formula (
1.16
).

∎

Appendix A
The Hilbert transform and the Cauchy integral

In this appendix, we provide some detailed analysis of the Hilbert transform and the Cauchy integral used in this paper. We start with some basic definitions.

Definition A.1
.

Let
\theta\in(0,\pi/2)
and
\omega\in\mathbb{C}
. We define
C_{\theta}(\omega)
as the cone

(A.1)

C_{\theta}(\omega):=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:Im\{z\}\leq\Im\{\omega\},\tan^{-1}(\frac{|\Re\{z-\omega\}|}{|\Im\{z-\omega|\}})\leq\theta/2\}.

Definition A.2
.

Given a chord arc
\gamma
parametrized by
\zeta
such that
\zeta-\alpha
is
2q\pi
periodic. Define the Cauchy integral as

(A.2)

C_{\gamma}f(z,t):=\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})f(\zeta)\,d\zeta

where
\int_{\gamma}
means integrating over a fundamental period of
\zeta-\alpha
.

With preparations above, we have the following properties of the Cauchy integral.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
f
and
\gamma
be sufficient nice functions, and
\gamma
has endpoints
\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}
. Assume that
Im\{\gamma_{L}\}=\Im\{\gamma_{R}\}
, and
f(\gamma_{L})=f(\gamma_{R})
. Let
\Omega
be the region below
\gamma
. We have the following conclusions:

(1)

Let
\omega\in\gamma
.Then one has that

(A.3)

C_{\gamma}f(z)\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})f(\omega)

as
z\rightarrow\omega
nontangentially.

(2)

If
f(\zeta)=G(\zeta)
, for some bounded holomorphic function
G
in
\Omega
such that
G(-q\pi+iy)=G(q\pi+iy)
, then

(A.4)

C_{\gamma}f(z)\rightarrow f(\omega)-\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{\gamma}f(\zeta)\,d\zeta

as
z\rightarrow\omega
nontangentially.

Proof.

Let
0<r\ll\frac{1}{q}
be fixed. Given
\omega\in\gamma
, we denote
\gamma_{1}=B_{r}(\omega)\cap\gamma
and
\gamma_{2}=\gamma-\gamma_{1}
. Again here, we abuse of notation that
\gamma
here means restricting onto a fundamental period of
\zeta-\alpha
after parameterizing the curve. Let
z\in C_{\theta}(\omega)
. For the integral over
\gamma_{2}
, by the continuity of
\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})
, we have

(A.5)

\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma_{2}}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})f(\zeta)\,d\zeta\rightarrow\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma_{2}}\cot(\frac{\omega-\zeta}{2q})f(\zeta)\,d\zeta,\quad\quad as\penalty\ z\rightarrow\omega.

On the other hand, for the integral over
\gamma_{1}
, we split it into two pieces:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma_{1}}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})f(\zeta)\,d\zeta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma_{1}}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})(f(\zeta)-f(\omega))\,d\zeta+\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma_{1}}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})f(\omega)\,d\zeta

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I+II.

For
I
, as
z\rightarrow\omega
in
C_{\theta}(\omega)
, we observe the followings:

1.

On
\gamma_{1}
, we have

(A.6)

\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})=\cos(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})\frac{\frac{z-\zeta}{2q}}{\sin(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})}\frac{2q}{z-\zeta}=\frac{2q}{z-\zeta}+O(1).

2.

|f(\zeta)-f(\omega)|\leq\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}|\zeta-\omega|
.

3.

For
z\in C_{\theta}(\omega)
, we have

(A.7)

c_{1}(\theta)|z-\zeta|\leq|\omega-\zeta|\leq c_{2}(\theta)|z-\zeta|,

where
c_{1}(\theta)
and
c_{2}(\theta)
depend continuously on
\theta
.

These facts imply

\displaystyle|I|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{\gamma_{1}}(\frac{2q}{|z-\zeta|}+O(1))\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}|\zeta-\omega||d\zeta|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{2}(\theta)\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{\gamma_{1}}(\frac{2q}{|z-\zeta|}+O(1))\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}|z-\zeta||d\zeta|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cr,

for some constant
C
depending on
\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\theta
, but with no dependence on
r
. Therefore as
r\rightarrow 0
,
I\rightarrow 0
(as
z\rightarrow\omega
).

Next we analyze
II
. Assume that
\gamma_{1}
has the starting point
\zeta_{L}
and the ending point
\zeta_{R}
. For
II
, since
\frac{1}{2q}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})=-\partial_{\zeta}\log\sin(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})
, for
|z-\omega|\ll r
(which is valid as
z\rightarrow\omega
), we have

\displaystyle II=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}(\log\sin\frac{z-\zeta_{L}}{2q}-\log\sin\frac{z-\zeta_{R}}{2q})f(\omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}(\log\sin\frac{\omega-\zeta_{L}}{2q}-\log\sin\frac{\omega-\zeta_{R}}{2q})f(\omega)

\displaystyle+\Big\{(\frac{1}{2\pi i}(\log\sin\frac{z-\zeta_{L}}{2q}-\log\sin\frac{z-\zeta_{R}}{2q}))-(\frac{1}{2\pi i}(\log\sin\frac{\omega-\zeta_{L}}{2q}-\log\sin\frac{\omega-\zeta_{R}}{2q}))\}f(\omega)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle f(\omega)(II_{1}+II_{2}).

It is straightforward to verify that for any
r>0
fixed,

(A.8)

|II_{2}|\leq C|z-\omega||\log\sin\frac{z-\zeta_{R}}{2q}|\rightarrow 0,\quad as\penalty\ z\rightarrow\omega.

For
II_{1}
,
we observe that as
r\rightarrow 0
, one has
\frac{\omega-\zeta_{L}}{\omega-\zeta_{R}}\rightarrow-1
. Therefore

\log\sin\frac{\omega-\zeta_{L}}{2q}-\log\sin\frac{\omega-\zeta_{R}}{2q}\rightarrow\log(-1)=i\pi\,\,\text{as}\,r\rightarrow 0.

Hence we obtain

(A.9)

II_{1}\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}

Let
r\rightarrow 0
, then (
A.3
) is proved.

To prove (
A.4
), suppose
f
is the boundary value of the holomorphic function
G
in
\Omega
. Take
0<r\ll 1/q
. Denote the left and right endpoints of
\gamma
by
\gamma_{L}
and
\gamma_{R}
, respectively. Taking
K\gg 1
, we set

Q_{L,K}:=\Re\{\gamma_{L}\}-iK,\quad\quad Q_{R,K}:=\Re\{\gamma_{R}\}-iK.

Let
\gamma_{1}
be the segment from
\gamma_{R}
to
Q_{R,K}
,
\gamma_{2}
be the segment from
Q_{R,K}
to
Q_{L,K}
, and
\gamma_{3}
be the segments from
Q_{L,K}
to
\gamma_{L}
. Let
\gamma_{4}
be
\partial B_{r}(z)
, oriented clockwisely. By Cauchy’s Theorem, since
f
is the boundary value of
G
in
\Omega
, we have

(A.10)

C_{\zeta}f(z)+\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma_{1}\cup\gamma_{2}\cup\gamma_{3}\cup\gamma_{4}}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})G(\zeta)\,d\zeta=0.

Note that

(A.11)

\int_{\gamma_{1}\cup\gamma_{3}}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})G(\zeta)\,d\zeta=0.

by the periodicity assumption and the orientation.

As
r\rightarrow 0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma_{4}}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})G(\zeta)\,d\zeta=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma_{4}}(\frac{2q}{z-\zeta}+O(1))G(\zeta)\,d\zeta\rightarrow-G(z).

Note that here for
\gamma_{4}
, the orientation is clockwise.

Notice that
\Im\{\zeta\}\rightarrow-\infty
, one has
\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})\rightarrow-i
. So for
K
large,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma_{2}}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})G(\zeta)\,d\zeta=-\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{\gamma_{2}}G(\zeta)\,d\zeta+O(K^{-1}).

By Cauchy’s Theorem again, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\int_{\gamma\cup\gamma_{1}\cup\gamma_{2}\cup\gamma_{3}}G(\zeta)\,d\zeta=0.

Putting everything together, one has

(A.12)

-\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{\gamma_{2}}G(\zeta)\,d\zeta=\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{\gamma}f(\zeta)\,d\zeta.

Therefore we conclude that

(A.13)

\lim_{z\rightarrow\omega}C_{\gamma}f(z)=f(\omega)-\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{\gamma}f(\zeta)\,d\zeta

as desired.
∎

As a consequence of Lemma (
A.1
), we obtain the following conclusion.

Corollary A.1
.

Let
f
and
\gamma
be sufficient nice functions, and suppose that
\gamma
has endpoints
\gamma_{L},\gamma_{R}
. Let
\Omega
be the region below
\gamma
. Assume that
\Im\{\gamma_{L}\}=\Im\{\gamma_{R}\}
, and
f(\gamma_{L})=f(\gamma_{R})
.

(a)

\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})f
is the boundary value of a periodic holomorphic function
\mathcal{F}
on
\Omega
, with
\mathcal{F}(z)\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}c_{0}
as
\Im\{z\}\rightarrow-\infty
.

(b)

f
is the boundary value of a holomorphic function
G
on
\Omega
satisfying
G(\Re\{\gamma_{L}\}+iy)=G(\Re\{\gamma_{R}\}+iy)
for all
y<\Im\{\gamma_{L}\}
and
G(x+iy)\rightarrow c_{0}
as
y\rightarrow-\infty
if and only if

(A.14)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})f=c_{0},

where
c_{0}=\frac{1}{2q\pi}\int_{\gamma}f(\zeta)\,d\zeta
.

Proof.

For (a), by Lemma
A.1
,
\mathcal{F}(z):=C_{\gamma}f(z)
has the boundary value
\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})f
, and
\mathcal{F}(z)\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}c_{0}
as
\Im\{z\}\rightarrow-\infty
.

For (b), in one direction, if
f
is the boundary value of a bounded holomorphic function in
\Omega
, then by Lemma
A.1
, we have

(A.15)

\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})f=f-\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{\gamma}f(\zeta)\,d\zeta,

which implies

(A.16)

(I-\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})f=\frac{1}{2q\pi}\int_{\gamma}f(\zeta)\,d\zeta.

On the other hand, if
(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})f=c_{0}
, then
\mathcal{H}_{\gamma}f=f-c_{0}
. Define
G(z)
by
G(z):=C_{\gamma}f(z)+\frac{1}{2}c_{0}
. The boundary value of
G(z)
is

\frac{1}{2}c_{0}+\frac{1}{2}(I+\mathcal{H}_{\gamma})f=\frac{1}{2}c_{0}+\frac{1}{2}f+(\frac{1}{2}f-\frac{1}{2}c_{0})=f.

Moreover, using that fact

\lim_{\Im\{z\}\rightarrow-\infty}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})=i,

we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\Im\{z\}\rightarrow-\infty}C_{\gamma}f(z)=\frac{1}{4q\pi i}\lim_{\Im\{z\}\rightarrow-\infty}\int_{\gamma}\cot(\frac{z-\zeta}{2q})f(\zeta)\,d\zeta=\frac{1}{4q\pi}\int_{\gamma}f(\zeta)\,d\zeta=\frac{1}{2}c_{0}.

So
G(z)\rightarrow c_{0}
as
\Im\{z\}\rightarrow-\infty
, and
G(z)
has the boundary value
f
.
∎

Appendix B
Identities

In this appendix, we provide the proof of Lemma
2.5
in details.

Proof of Lemma
2.5
.

For (
2.18
), performing integration by parts, we rewrite the Hilbert transform as

(B.1)

\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}f(\alpha,t)=\frac{1}{\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\log(\sin(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})f_{\beta}\,d\beta.

By direct computations, one has

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}f(\alpha,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\zeta_{\alpha}\cot(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})f_{\beta}\,d\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\zeta_{\alpha}\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}f.

Therefore, we can conclude that

\displaystyle[\partial_{\alpha},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]f=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}f-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}\partial_{\alpha}f=[\zeta_{\beta},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{f_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}},

which gives (
2.18
) and then (
2.19
) is an easy consequence of (
2.18
).

For (
2.20
), using (
B.1
), we get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}f(\alpha,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(\zeta_{t}(\alpha,t)-\zeta_{t}(\beta,t))\cot(\frac{\zeta(\alpha,t)-\zeta(\beta,t)}{2q})f_{\beta}\,d\beta+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}f_{t}.

by direct differentiation.
So we obtain

\displaystyle[\partial_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]f=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}f-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}f_{t}=[\zeta_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{f_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}},

which gives (
2.20
). And then (
2.19
) and (
2.20
) together implies (
2.21
).

For (
2.22
), we note that

(B.2)

[D_{t}^{2},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]=D_{t}[D_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]+[D_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]D_{t}.

We first calculate
\partial_{t}[z_{t},\mathcal{H}_{z}]g
. Notice that

\partial_{t}\cot(\frac{z(\alpha,t)-z(\beta,t)}{2q})=-\frac{1}{2q}\frac{z_{t}(\alpha,t)-z_{t}(\beta,t)}{\sin(\frac{z(\alpha,t)-z(\beta,t)}{2q})}.

By direct computations, one has

\displaystyle\partial_{t}[z_{t},\mathcal{H}_{z}]g=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(z_{t}(\alpha,t)-z_{t}(\beta,t))\cot(\frac{z(\alpha,t)-z(\beta,t)}{2q})g_{\beta}(\beta,t)\,d\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(z_{t}(\alpha,t)-z_{t}(\beta,t))\cot(\frac{z(\alpha,t)-z(\beta,t)}{2q})g_{\beta}(\beta,t)\,d\beta

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi q^{2}i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{z_{t}(\alpha)-z_{t}(\beta)}{\sin(\frac{1}{2q}(z(\alpha)-z(\beta)))}\Big)^{2}g_{\beta}\,d\beta

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(z_{t}(\alpha,t)-z_{t}(\beta,t))\cot(\frac{z(\alpha,t)-z(\beta,t)}{2q})\partial_{t}g_{\beta}(\beta,t)\,d\beta

Changing of variables, we obtain

\displaystyle D_{t}[D_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]f=

\displaystyle[D_{t}^{2}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{f_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{4\pi q^{2}i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\sin(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)))}\Big)^{2}f_{\beta}\,d\beta+[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}f}{\zeta_{\alpha}}.

Moreover, note that

\displaystyle D_{t}[D_{t},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]f=

\displaystyle D_{t}[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{f_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}.

Therefore we have

(B.3)

[D_{t}^{2},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]f=[D_{t}^{2}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{f_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}+2[D_{t}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}f}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{4\pi q^{2}i}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}\Big(\frac{D_{t}\zeta(\alpha)-D_{t}\zeta(\beta)}{\sin(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta(\alpha)-\zeta(\beta)))}\Big)^{2}f_{\beta}\,d\beta,

which is (
2.22
).

By (
2.19
), one has

\displaystyle[D_{t}^{2}\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{f_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}-[iA\partial_{\alpha},\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]f=[(D_{t}^{2}-iA\partial_{\alpha})\zeta,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{f_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}=[-i,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}]\frac{f_{\alpha}}{\zeta_{\alpha}}=0.

Hence putting things together, we conclude (
2.23
).
∎

Appendix C
Supplementary proofs

In this section, we provide supplementary proofs of some lemmata used in the main part of the paper.

C.1.
Proof of Lemma
7.5

Proof of Lemma
7.5
.

Recall that

\rho:=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})\Big[\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST})\Big],

where

\theta:=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\zeta-\alpha)=2(\zeta-\alpha)-(\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}}+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\zeta-\alpha),

\theta_{ST}=(I-\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})(\zeta_{ST}-\alpha)=2(\zeta_{ST}-\alpha)-(\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}}+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})(\zeta_{ST}-\alpha),

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}:=

\displaystyle(I-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})(\tilde{\zeta}-\alpha)-(I-\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}})(\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(\tilde{\zeta}-\alpha)-(\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}}+\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})(\tilde{\zeta}-\alpha)-\Big(2(\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-\alpha)-(\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}}+\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}})(\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-\alpha)\Big)+e,

where by the explicit construction of
\tilde{\zeta}
and
\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}
,

(C.1)

\left\lVert e\right\rVert_{H^{s^{\prime}}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{7/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Taking the difference, it follows

\displaystyle\theta-\theta_{ST}-(\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\theta}_{ST})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(\zeta-\zeta_{ST}-(\tilde{\zeta}-\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}))-e-g,

where

(C.2)

g:=(\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta}}+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta})(\zeta-\alpha)-(\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}}}+\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{ST}})(\zeta_{ST}-\alpha)-\Big((\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}}}+\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}})(\tilde{\zeta}-\alpha)-(\overline{\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}}}+\mathcal{H}_{\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}})(\tilde{\zeta}_{ST}-\alpha)\Big).

By manipulating the differences, it is easy to obtain

(C.3)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}g\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Combing computations and estimates above, one has

(C.4)

\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\rho-2r)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

Finally by the same argument we obtain

(C.5)

\left\lVert D_{t}\rho-2D_{t}r\right\rVert_{H^{s+1/2}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C(\epsilon E_{s}^{1/2}+\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}\epsilon^{5/2}),

and

(C.6)

\left\lVert D_{t}(D_{t}\rho-2D_{t}r)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{5/2}\delta e^{\epsilon^{2}t}.

We are done.
∎

C.2.
Proof of Proposition
2.2

Proof.

To suppress notations, we write
\zeta_{j}(\alpha,t)
as
\zeta_{j}(\alpha)
.

Using elementary trigonometric identities

\displaystyle\cos(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta_{1}(\alpha)-\zeta_{1}(\beta)))\sin(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta_{2}(\alpha)-\zeta_{2}(\beta)))

\displaystyle-\cos(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta_{2}(\alpha)-\zeta_{2}(\beta)))\sin(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta_{1}(\alpha)-\zeta_{1}(\beta)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin(\frac{1}{2q}((\zeta_{1}(\alpha)-\zeta_{1}(\beta))-(\zeta_{2}(\alpha)-\zeta_{2}(\beta)))),

we can write the difference as

\displaystyle\Big(S_{\zeta_{1}}-S_{\zeta_{2}}\Big)(f,g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[g,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{1}}]\frac{f_{\alpha}}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{1}}-[g,\mathcal{H}_{\zeta_{2}}]\frac{f_{\alpha}}{\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))\Big(\frac{1}{\tan(\frac{1}{2q}\Big(\zeta_{1}(\alpha)-\zeta_{1}(\beta)))}-\frac{1}{\tan(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta_{2}(\alpha)-\zeta_{2}(\beta)))}\Big)f_{\beta}(\beta)\,d\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))\frac{\sin(\frac{1}{2q}((\zeta_{1}(\alpha)-\zeta_{1}(\beta))-(\zeta_{2}(\alpha)-\zeta_{2}(\beta))))}{\sin(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta_{1}(\alpha)-\zeta_{1}(\beta)))\sin(\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta_{2}(\alpha)-\zeta_{2}(\beta)))}f_{\beta}\,d\beta.

By (
2.37
), we have

(C.7)

\displaystyle\left\lVert(S_{\zeta_{1}}-S_{\zeta_{2}}\Big)(f,g)\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle C\|\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})\|_{Y}\|f\|_{Z}\|g\|_{Y}.

Next we analyze (
2.41
). We first introduce the notation:

(C.8)

L_{j}(\alpha,\beta):=\frac{1}{2q}(\zeta_{j}(\alpha)-\zeta_{j}(\beta)).

Then we perform some elementary computations. Using trigonometric identities again, we have

\displaystyle\cot L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)-\cot L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)-\Big\{\cot L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-\cot L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sin(L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))}{\sin L_{1}(\alpha,\beta))\sin L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)}-\frac{\sin(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta))}{\sin L_{3}(\alpha,\beta))\sin L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{1}(\alpha,\beta)+K_{2}(\alpha,\beta),

where we define

(C.9)

\displaystyle K_{1}(\alpha,\beta)=\frac{\sin\Big(L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)\Big)-\sin\Big(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta))\Big)}{\sin L_{1}(\alpha,\beta))\sin L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)},

(C.10)

\displaystyle K_{2}(\alpha,\beta)=\sin(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta))\Big(\frac{1}{\sin L_{1}(\alpha,\beta))\sin L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)}-\frac{1}{\sin L_{3}(\alpha,\beta))\sin L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)}\Big).

We write
\sin L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)\sin L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)
as

(C.11)

\displaystyle\sin L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)\sin L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)=\frac{1}{2}\cos(L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))-\frac{1}{2}\cos(L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)),

and similarly

(C.12)

\displaystyle\sin L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)\sin L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)=\frac{1}{2}\cos(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta))-\frac{1}{2}\cos(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)),

Taking the difference of to expressions above, we have

\displaystyle\sin L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)\sin L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)-\sin L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)\sin L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\cos(L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))-\frac{1}{2}\cos(L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{1}{2}\cos(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta))-\frac{1}{2}\cos(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{4}(\alpha,\beta))\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sin((L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))-(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)))

\displaystyle\times\sin((L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))+(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)))

\displaystyle+\sin((L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))-(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)))

\displaystyle\times\sin((L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))+(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)))

We further decompose
K_{2}(\alpha,\beta)
as

(C.13)

K_{2}(\alpha,\beta)=K_{21}(\alpha,\beta)+K_{22}(\alpha,\beta),

where

(C.14)

\begin{split}K_{21}(\alpha,\beta)=&-\sin(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta))\\
&\times\frac{\sin((L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))-(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)))}{\sin L_{1}(\alpha,\beta))\sin L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)}\\
&\times\frac{\sin((L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))+(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)))}{\sin L_{3}(\alpha,\beta))\sin L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)},\end{split}

and

(C.15)

\begin{split}K_{22}(\alpha,\beta)=&\sin(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)-L_{4}(\alpha,\beta))\\
&\times\frac{\sin((L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))-(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)))}{\sin L_{1}(\alpha,\beta))\sin L_{2}(\alpha,\beta)}\\
&\times\frac{\sin((L_{1}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{2}(\alpha,\beta))+(L_{3}(\alpha,\beta)+L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)))}{\sin L_{3}(\alpha,\beta))\sin L_{4}(\alpha,\beta)}.\end{split}

Now we can write the difference of commutators from (
2.41
) as

(C.16)

\displaystyle\Big(S_{\zeta_{1}}-S_{\zeta_{2}}-(S_{\zeta_{3}}-S_{\zeta_{4}})\Big)(f,g)

(C.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))[K_{1}(\alpha,\beta)+K_{21}(\alpha,\beta)+K_{22}(\alpha,\beta)]f_{\beta}\,d\beta

By the explicit form of
K_{1}
,
K_{21}
, and
K_{22}
and (
2.37
), we have

(C.18)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))K_{1}(\alpha,\beta)f_{\beta}\,d\beta\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}\leq C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}-(\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{4})\right\rVert_{Y}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{Y}\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{Z}.

Secondly, one can estimate

(C.19)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))K_{21}(\alpha,\beta)f_{\beta}\,d\beta\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

(C.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{4})\|_{Y}\|\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}-(\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{4}))\|_{Y}\Big(\|\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})+\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{4})\|_{Y}\|f\|_{Z}

(C.21)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}-(\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{4}))\right\rVert_{Y}\Big(\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})\right\rVert_{Y}+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{3})\right\rVert_{Y}\Big)^{2}\|f\|_{Z}\|g\|_{Y}.

Finally, we have

(C.22)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{2q\pi i}\text{p.v.}\int_{-q\pi}^{q\pi}(g(\alpha)-g(\beta))K_{22}(\alpha,\beta)f_{\beta}\,d\beta\right\rVert_{H^{s}(q\mathbb{T})}

(C.23)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{4})\|_{Y}\|\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{1}+\zeta_{2}-(\zeta_{3}+\zeta_{4}))\|_{Y}\Big(\|\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{1}+\zeta_{2}+\zeta_{3}+\zeta_{4})\|_{Y}\|f\|_{Z}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{4})\|_{Y}\Big(\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{3})\right\rVert_{Y}+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}(\zeta_{2}-\zeta_{4})\right\rVert_{Y}\Big)\sum_{j=1}^{4}\Big(1+\left\lVert\partial_{\alpha}\zeta_{j}-1\right\rVert_{Y}\Big)\|f\|_{Z}\|g\|_{Y}.

Putting three estimates above together, we obtain the desired result.
∎

C.3.
Proof of Proposition
2.4

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is purely algebraic. Firstly, we regroup the expression we are interested as

\displaystyle\Big(S_{\zeta_{1}}(g_{1},f_{1})-S_{\zeta_{2}}(g_{2},f_{2})\Big)-\Big(S_{\zeta_{3}}(g_{3},f_{3})-S_{\zeta_{4}}(g_{4},f_{4})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{\zeta_{1}}(g_{1},f_{1}-f_{2}-(f_{3}-f_{4}))
